<result>### Verdict: ACCEPT

Head reviewed: `b8c2442c4fc4899a877847e5648c7ee315c20e5c`, which is `origin/v1/v114-wp1316-auto-inputs` and the worktree HEAD. Previous head `c3b260fd` is the delta base. Read-only review: git, rg and file reads only, nothing built or run. All findings below are non-blocking.

### Blockers
No blockers found.

**BLOCKER 1 (checkout trust): resolved.**
- Every host-side git call in the new code is behind `trustedGitArgs` or `verifyCheckout`, with `--git-dir`/`--work-tree` prepended. I checked each `execFileSync` in the three files:
  - `candidateDiff`'s `git()` closure, `specialist_inputs.ts:299`, with `pin` set at `:311` before any call. It also serves `snapshotSupport`, `driverRefusal` and `protectedMatcher`.
  - `attributesTree`, `:253`, takes `pin`.
  - The operator-side listing, `:412`, uses `verifyCheckout(operator)` and refuses on a reason. Skipping the nested-repo check there is deliberate, because the operator's checkout is not agent-writable.
  - `plainCandidateDiff`, `:433`, `:438` and the `driverCheck` at `:449`.
  - `snapshotCandidate`, `operation_git.ts:440`, calls `trustedGitArgs` before `mkdtemp`. Its `run()` and `attributesTree` both use the pin, and `commit-tree` goes through `run`.
  - `operation_runner_execution.ts` has no direct git call. It only reaches the two builders above.
- The `copyFileSync` of the unit's index now reads from the verified git dir, which the agent cannot write.
- The red-first tests would fail without the fix:
  - **Replaced `.git` pointer:** the replaced-pointer test needs a "point back" refusal, which the old code never produced. It also asserts the result is not a domain refusal.
  - **Nested `.git`:** the old code skipped a nested repo, so it returned no error from the diff and no throw from the snapshot. The planted-repo test in `specialist_inputs_domain.test.ts` and the rewritten one in `specialist_inputs_hardening.test.ts` both assert the refusal.
- TOCTOU between `verifyCheckout` and use is acceptable. Git runs against the pinned dir, and the unit's model is not running while the diff or snapshot is built.

**BLOCKER 2 (reviewer fallback): resolved.**
- `reviewEvidence` (`operation_runner_execution.ts:732`) falls back to `plainCandidateDiff` only when the error carries `unsupported`. Only `snapshotSupport` sets that field, and it runs after trust verification, so an untrusted checkout can never take the fallback.
- The reviewer's diff gets a "plain git diff" note carrying the reason, and it is run through `redactForPersistence`.
- The plain builder keeps the protections:
  - It verifies and pins the checkout.
  - It builds from a base-tree scratch index, so assume-unchanged and skip-worktree flags cannot hide files.
  - It runs `check-attr` driver refusal before any diff.
  - It filters protected paths and applies the protected-content comparison.
  - Once any protected path changes, every new file is omitted.
- A reviewer test runs through every `REFUSED` state plus the subdirectory workspace. It asserts no `diffError`, a "plain git diff" note and the changed marker present. Another test covers protected-file and moved-file omission, and a replaced `.git`.

**SMALLER items:**
- (b) Resolved. A `.gitattributes` over 1 MiB or unreadable makes the predicate refuse, at `specialist_inputs.ts:140-144`. A test covers the large file, and a deleted `.gitattributes` is correctly tolerated.
- (c) Resolved. A U+FFFD path is refused, at `:131-133`, and the test uses a raw 0xFF name.
- (d) Resolved. The `ownIndex` fallback runs only on ENOENT, at `operation_git.ts:464-468`.
- (e) Resolved. The snapshot is built before the old worktree and branch are removed (`:210`). A test confirms the old worktree survives a failed rebuild.
- (f) Resolved. A4a records that `add -A` snapshots and diffs leave out ignored files.
- (g) Resolved. The sweep regex now accepts only `/automatic inputs are not available/`. The snapshot refusal tests now cover all of `REFUSED` (merge, submodule and `core.*` were previously excluded). The refusal test also asserts the `unsupported` marker.
- (h) Resolved.
  - **A4:** it now says "on git 2.40+ (older git ignores `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE` silently; see A4a)", the stale "(superseded…)" parenthetical is gone, and the OPEN block moved out to its own row.
  - **A4a:** it is a separate OPEN row.
  - **Other rows:** the FINDINGS diff is +2/-1, only the A4 row and the new A4a row, so all other rows are intact.
- (a) Partly resolved:
  - The false "before 2.38" claim is gone, and the catch now rethrows anything that is not a recognised message (`specialist_inputs.ts:104-108`).
  - That catch is probably dead code; see Suggestions.

### Suggestions
- **Medium (silent evidence gap in the fallback)** — `specialist_inputs.ts:450-470`.
  - **Trigger:** a unit modifies a tracked file whose path is not valid UTF-8. The predicate refuses it, then `plainCandidateDiff` decodes the `-z` listing as UTF-8 and the `:(literal)` pathspec matches nothing.
  - **Effect:** git does not error. The reviewer gets a non-empty diff labelled "plain" that quietly lacks that file. Main showed it. A4a discloses this, but the in-prompt note does not.
  - **Fix:** in the plain builder, return `{ error }` or count it as omitted when any `tracked` or `untracked` path contains `\uFFFD`.
- **Medium (non-predicate refusals still newly block reviewers, relative to main)** — `operation_runner_execution.ts:732`.
  - **Cases:** an untrusted checkout, a driver refusal, more than 2,000 paths, a nested repo in a linked unit worktree (`trustedGitArgs` refuses it), and the "no starting commit" guard in `candidateDiffFor` all block the review. Main served all of these, because `reviewCandidateDiff` had no trust check, path cap or guard.
  - **Why it is not a blocker:** this is outside the predicate-refusal fallback you asked about, and A4a states it honestly. It is still a regression for ordinary large reviews, for example 2,000+ paths of vendored or generated files.
  - **Fix:** raise the cap for the plain path, or let the reviewer see a truncated diff with a note. This is the owner's call.
- **Low (likely dead code and a misleading refusal on old git)** — `specialist_inputs.ts:104-109`.
  - **Basis:** from memory of git's behaviour, not run here, so I put confidence at about 0.7. `git rev-parse` before 2.38 echoes an unknown `--flag` to stdout and exits 0.
  - **Effect:** `format` becomes `"--show-object-format"`, so the user gets the refusal "uses the --show-object-format object format". The `unknown option` catch is never reached.
  - **Fix:** treat any `format` other than `sha1` or `sha256` as "git too old to read the object format", or require a minimum git version up front.
- **Low (pre-existing, adjacent)** — `operation_git.ts:33-38` and `:286-315`.
  - **What it is:** `gitResult` runs `status`, `add -A` and `commit` in `entry.worktree` with no trust check. This is the same agent-writable worktree the PR now guards for the snapshot, and it is on main unchanged.
  - **Fix:** track it as a follow-up and route it through `trustedGitArgs`.
- **Low (prompt hygiene)** — `operation_runner_execution.ts:735`. The plain-diff note embeds `candidate.unsupported`, which can include an agent-chosen path (up to 80 characters, for example a submodule path), unquoted in the reviewer prompt. Render the reason with `JSON.stringify`, as the other model-influenced strings are.

- **Misattached JSDoc:** the `snapshotSupport` doc (`specialist_inputs.ts:72-89`) attaches to `const MAX_ATTRIBUTES_BYTES`. The `candidateDiff` doc (`:259-287`) attaches to `interface CandidateDiff`.
- **Prose weight:** the long narrative comments, plus the A4 row at roughly 6 KB in a single cell, add review burden.

- **Plain builder:** `plainCandidateDiff` partly duplicates `candidateDiff`. It shares `driverRefusal`, `protectedMatcher` and `GIT_SAFE`, which is reasonable.

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT** at head `dfa28c45be8632af1f4dfe33e577b189ed0617b6`. I found no Blocker or Medium. There are 4 Low findings, and one of them is the open Entire Low. I did not build or run anything. The worktree is at dfa28c45 (detached).

I read the new-head diff against 00ea64be plus `snapshotSupport`, `undecodablePath`, `protectedContent`, `protectedMatcher`, `candidateDiff` and `plainCandidateDiff` in full. I also checked the `operation_git.ts:462` and `operation_runner_execution.ts:51,733` call sites.

### Blockers
No blockers found.

### Findings

**1. Low — `cli/src/core/specialist_inputs.ts` ~442, stale `plainCandidateDiff` doc (the open Entire Low)**
- The comment reads "tracked changes against the base (`git diff`, no copy detection, no scratch index) plus untracked files". "No scratch index" has been false since the base-tree index existed, and this commit makes it more false. The builder does `read-tree` of the base into a private index (`nexus-plain-index-*`). It then removes the real index's staged deletions from that index with `update-index --force-remove`, and runs `git diff &lt;commit&gt;` against it.
- It should say something like: "tracked changes against the base (`git diff &lt;commit&gt;` through a private index holding the base tree minus the real index's staged removals; `--no-renames`, no copy detection) plus untracked files". It should also state that every path source (base tree, index, untracked) must be valid UTF-8 or the builder returns an error.
- The same comment's "a new file carrying protected content is omitted" should add "or a protected source that cannot be compared".

**2. Low — `specialist_inputs.ts:244-248` (also 233-235 in `protectedContent`), lstat then `readFileSync` follows symlinks**
- Trigger: a protected or new path REDACTEDes `lstat().isFile()`. The agent then swaps it for a symlink, either the final component or a parent directory, before `readFileSync`. The read follows the link to a file outside the repo (for example `/etc/shadow`), and only an in-memory hash and line set is kept.
- Nothing is shown to the reviewer, and the window needs the agent to run concurrently with the synchronous builder. It still breaks your rule that a file outside the repo must never be read.
- Fix: open with `fs.openSync(p, O_RDONLY | O_NOFOLLOW | O_NONBLOCK)`, `fstat` the fd and require `isFile()` and the size bound, then read from the fd. The same fix stops a swap to a FIFO from blocking the read. If you accept the window, state it in the FINDINGS Protected-content comparison paragraph.

**3. Low — `specialist_inputs.ts:118-124`, wrong remediation hint for old git**
- The catch for `ambiguous argument|unknown option|unknown revision` now leaves `format=""`, which gives `refuse("git cannot report this repository's object format", "no workaround")`. That is the git-before-2.38 case, and there the workaround is to upgrade git.
- Before this commit the hint was "upgrade git". The test now only asserts `/cannot report/`, so the lost hint is unguarded.
- Fix: use the hint "upgrade git (2.38+) or check the repository" for the non-sha answer, and keep "no workaround" for sha256.

**4. Low — `specialist_inputs.ts:247`, `carriedBy` omits every new non-regular file**
- While any readable protected source exists, a new symlink to an in-repo file, or any other non-regular new file, is dropped and counted in `omittedNew`. This is correct fail-closed behaviour and is covered by the dlnk test.
- The FINDINGS row does not mention that every new symlink is omitted. Add one clause to it.
- Related over-omission: a symlinked `.env` in the operator checkout (`protectedMatcher`, the operator loop) disables new-file display for every unit. That is accepted and documented as fail-closed.

### (1) Ways protected content can still reach the auto-collected inputs
The comparison skips and early returns (`specialist_inputs.ts:211-262`):

| Path / skip in the code | Result |
|---|---|
| Name contains U+FFFD | `complete=false` |
| Symlink, special file, directory (non-`.nexus`) | `complete=false` |
| Symlink inside `.nexus` | Skipped on purpose. The target is itself a source if protected. |
| Dangling symlink | lstat succeeds but it is not a file, so `complete=false` |
| Symlink chain | Same, never dereferenced |
| ENOENT | Skipped. A deleted protected path shows up as a D group, so `protectedChanged` fires (the plain builder reaches the same result through `tracked`). |
| ENOTDIR, ELOOP, EACCES and other errors | `complete=false` |
| Source over 4 MiB or total over 64 MiB | `complete=false` |
| Source read throws | `complete=false` |
| `.nexus` file over the bound | Skipped on purpose |

- **New-file side (`carriedBy`):**
  - It returns true when `!complete`.
  - It returns true for a non-regular new file, a file over 16 MiB, or a read error.
  - It returns false early only when no readable source exists. In that case there is nothing to leak, because a symlinked `.env` sets `complete=false`.
- **Case-insensitive collisions:** the main builder refuses `core.ignorecase`. The plain fallback does not, and it relies on `inputPathProtection` lowercasing only the `.nexus` segment. It is a residual, and it is untested.
- **Out of scope, deliberately:**
  - Encoded, base64, quoted or values-only copies, and lines under 8 characters.
  - Lines pasted into an already-existing (M) file.
  - Commented secret lines.
  - A move of an ignored, untracked `.env` (`mv .env notes.txt`). git sees no D and no source remains on disk, so it looks like an ordinary new file. Only `redactForPersistence` guards it. This pre-dates the change and is worth one line in the doc.
- **Hardlinks:** these are regular files and are compared.
- **Parent-directory symlinks:** git never lists paths through a symlink, so only the TOCTOU in finding 2 applies.
- **Reading outside the repo:** the code never deliberately dereferences. The only way to read outside is the swap in finding 2.

### (2) Staged removals and path validation
- **Staged removals are preserved.** `diff-index --cached --name-status -z --no-renames &lt;commit&gt;` run on the real index yields `D` pairs, which `update-index --force-remove -z --stdin` removes from the scratch index. `git diff &lt;commit&gt;` (tree against worktree) then reports the deletion for a path that stays on disk. The path lands in `tracked`, is protected-checked, and sets `protectedChanged` if protected. Its on-disk copy is still a source through the ignored or untracked listing.
- **Other staged states are fine.** Staged-new files fall out of the scratch index and are handled as untracked. Unmerged `U` entries are left alone and show up as M.
- **No deleted file is silently omitted in any path source.**
  - `undecodablePath` covers ls-tree of the base commit (so a deleted undecodable name is caught), the real index, and untracked files.
  - Ignored sources with undecodable names make the comparison incomplete.
  - The `diff`-reported `tracked` list can only contain names from the tree or the index.
  - It is called in `snapshotSupport` (which now resolves `base`) and in `plainCandidateDiff`.
- **Cost:** `ls-files` now runs about twice in `snapshotSupport` and several times in the plain builder. That is a minor performance cost.

### (3) Trust boundary
- Every new git call goes through `pin` (`trustedGitArgs`) plus `GIT_SAFE`, `ownEnv` or the scratch env, or the `run` closure of the caller.
- `diff-index`, `update-index --force-remove`, `ls-tree` and `ls-files` read no worktree content, so no filter or driver can run. All of them run before `driverCheck` and are harmless there.
- `--end-of-options` is used before the commit in both diffs.
- No regression found.

### (4) Tests
- **Symlink fix:**
  - The table is 9 source shapes by 3 copy shapes, and each row runs both builders.
  - The dedicated symlink test asserts no error, no LEAK, `unrelated.txt` omitted and `omittedNew &gt;= 2`.
  - It is red at 00ea64be: with the old `!isFile → continue`, `notes.txt` was shown.
- **Staged removal:** the filemode=false test asserts `deleted file mode` for `generated.txt`. It is red on the old base-index-only code.
- **Undecodable name:** the test covers a name deleted from the base and fails if the builder returns a diff instead of an error.
- **Weaknesses:**
  - The table accepts any `error` as REDACTEDing, via `assert.match(error, /automatic inputs…|git could not produce/)` then `continue`, so a regression into an error would still REDACTED. Assert `!("error" in r)` for rows where support is expected, as the dedicated test does.
  - The "directory in its place" row probably exercises the regular-file path (`.env/inner.txt` is listed as a file), not the `!isFile` branch.
  - There are no tests for a parent-directory symlink, the ENOENT skip, or the old-git hint.

### Good
- A single fail-closed predicate replaces per-state special cases.
- One shared `undecodablePath` helper is used at every site.
- `snapshotSupport` now resolves the caller's `base`, not HEAD.
- The FINDINGS row is updated to match the code, including residuals and out-of-scope items.
- The plain builder now diffs against an explicit commit.

### Slop: Low
- The two strongest reasons:
  - The core fix is the right shape and stays small. It reuses `complete`, `protectedChanged` and the shared helper instead of adding parallel mechanisms.
  - The tests are broad and genuinely red-first.
- The remaining issues are isolated: the stale doc comment, the lost "upgrade git" hint, an overly permissive error branch in the table test, and repeated `ls-files` calls.

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**REQUEST_CHANGES on 463a0c5e68055943c0e3ce098cd3060c6fab9988.** Redaction is still monotone and the cost is linear. But finding (1) is only fixed for look-alike runs that `isBase64KeyRun` rejects. A leading blob of look-alikes that it accepts still starves a later real key. I read the code only and ran nothing.

### Blockers

- **Low (the original Entire finding, still open for the common form)** — `cli/src/core/egress.ts:605-608`, with the shared budget at `egress.ts:1109` and the deterministic-pattern charge at `egress.ts:1148`.
  - **Trigger:** `sk-%2F` units whose body `isBase64KeyRun` accepts are still charged. These are units with a digit or capital that are not path-like, e.g. `sk-%2F0000000a-sk-%2F0000000b-…`. The existing test `extensions cannot make the values to replace much larger than the text` (`redactor_encoded_dotted.test.ts:~150`) builds exactly this shape. It asserts `total &gt; input.length` because "the extensions are accepted until the budget is spent".
  - **Why it starves:** Units are joined by `-`, which is a key character, so every `sk-` start sits at a word boundary. Each start gets its own 1024-character window and is charged up to ~1 KB. Rescans stop extending only once the 16n ceiling is spent, which is far later than the extension budget (`text.length + 1024`).
  - **How cheap it is:** About 100 sixteen-character units charge ~100 KB. Add filler so the text is ~100 KB, then a real key `sk-…%2F….&lt;TAIL&gt;`. `chargeExtension` returns false for the real key, only the plain key is redacted, and the dotted tail stays in clear. This is the same leak as before, reached with a blob that is accepted rather than rejected. The new test only covers the rejected blob (`sk-%2F-`, which has no digit or capital), so it REDACTEDes while this variant is open.
  - **Fix:** Don't let overlapping rescans spend the main budget. Charge the main budget only for extensions of fresh keys (`at &gt;= scannedTo`), or charge only the part beyond the furthest point already charged. Give rescans (`at &lt; scannedTo`) a separate small quota, and drop their extensions once that is spent.
  - **Fix, deterministic loop:** Give that loop its own budget, so a blob in `scanBase64Keys` can't starve the dotted extensions at `egress.ts:1148`.
  - **Test:** Add a red-first test using the accepted blob, e.g. `sk-%2F${i.toString(36).padStart(8,"A")}-` ×N (about 100 units is enough with filler) followed by a real key. Assert that no tail remains.

- **Low (the corner the commit admits, trigger confirmed)** — `egress.ts:586-610`.
  - **Trigger:** A dense blob of about 11% of the text spends the 16n ceiling. A real key follows with no separator, inside the window the blob last scanned, and padding after it takes the text to about 9 times the blob length.
  - **Severity:** The plain key is still redacted and only the dotted tail leaks, so Low. The fix above largely removes it as well, because rescans would stop drawing on the shared pool. With the current design it is a documented limitation, not a regression.

### Verified

- **(a) Monotone, no leak against origin/main.**
  - `runEnd` is always in the `Set([runEnd, encodedEnd, extendedEnd])` and is never charged (`end &gt; runEnd` guards the charge).
  - When `mayExtend` is false, `extendedEnd = runEnd`.
  - In the pattern loop, `ends = [end]` is always pushed. The extended span is added only if `chargeExtension` succeeds.
  - The `extensionBudget.left &gt; 0 ? extendDottedKey : end` line only skips work.
  - `chargedTo` charges each longer run only for what the shorter one did not take.
- **(b) No blow-up.**
  - Each `extendDottedKey` call is bounded by MAX_EXTENSION, and each segment scan is bounded by `limit`.
  - Fresh starts (`at &gt;= scannedTo`) scan new characters, so their total is at most n.
  - Rescans are capped by `rescanLeft`, with at most one overshoot of about 2 KB.
  - `matchEncodedKeyChar` uses a sticky regex, so there is no forward scan.
  - The values to replace are still bounded by the budget.
  - For `sk-sk-sk-` the run is skipped (`from = runEnd`), and `sk-a sk-a` and long dotted chains are bounded by the per-call window.
- **(c) The new test fails on the old code.** The old code charged the budget for every scanned window, rejected or not. 40000 rescans of ~1 KB exhaust `n + 1024`, so the later key lost its extension and the `after` assertions would fail. It also checks time and total value size. As noted above, it covers only the rejected blob.
- **(d) README is accurate.** `--worktree` and `--worktree-branch &lt;name&gt;` are opt-in flags in `cli/src/index.ts` and `sidecar.ts`, and `docs/WORKTREES.md` agrees. There are no other "by default" overclaims in README.md or docs/README.md. The only hit is "Approval by Default" at README.md:49, which describes an actual unset-mode default.
  - **Nit:** the new bullet omits that `--worktree` has a sandbox requirement (WORKTREES.md:34).
- **(e) The picker tests are meaningful.**
  - The 40x10 test asserts the title, a model row and the `╰` bottom border at 40x10. That does show the panel fits without being clipped, even without the row-count and width assertions. Keeping the "does_not_panic" name is fine.
  - The height test asserts a literal 11 on the rendered border span instead of restating the formula. The literal is brittle if the catalog changes, but it is honest.
  - Neither test is tautological or vacuous now.

### Slop: Low

The change is small and focused, and the tests assert observable behaviour. The two main points against it are comment noise and the incomplete fix:
- The new multi-clause comments and the long doc-comment line in `scanBase64Keys` are over-long and narrate rationale.
- The test and comment claim the starvation is fixed, but only the rejected-blob case is.

- `cli/test/redactor_encoded_dotted.test.ts`
- `README.md`

## What the pushes changed
- **Supported domain.** `snapshotSupport` in `cli/src/core/specialist_inputs.ts` is a single predicate used by `candidateDiff` and the debugger snapshot. It refuses sparse checkouts, skip-worktree and assume-unchanged entries, `core.filemode=false`, `core.symlinks=false`, `core.ignorecase=true`, submodules, unresolved merges, and subdirectory workspaces. It also refuses SHA-256 repositories, `filter=` attributes, oversized or unreadable `.gitattributes`, and non-UTF-8 paths in the base tree, index or untracked files.
- **Checkout trust.** Both builders and the new plain diff run git only after `trustedGitArgs`, against the pinned git dir and work tree.
- **Reviewer fallback.** `plainCandidateDiff` serves `reviewer` and `acceptance_reviewer` when the predicate refuses, and tells the reviewer it is a plain diff. It keeps staged removals from the real index.
- **Protected-content comparison.** A protected source that is a symlink, special file, directory, oversized or unreadable now omits every new file. Sources are read through a no-follow descriptor (`openRegular`).
- **Other fixes.** A failed rebuild of a stale snapshot leaves the old worktree in place. The index copy falls back to HEAD only on ENOENT.
- **Docs.** `docs/v1.1.4/FINDINGS.md` has A4 as FIXED and a new A4a as an OPEN accepted limitation for release review.

## Test status
- The touched suites REDACTED locally, including 105 tests in `specialist_inputs_domain.test.ts`.
- `context_auto_compaction` (anti-thrashing) fails identically on `origin/main`, so it is not from this PR.
- `specialist_roles_runtime` lingers after its tests REDACTED, also on `origin/main`.
- Red-first was shown for the symlink and staged-removal cases and the undecodable-name case (each failed on the prior head). The first predicate tests were written after the code.

## Residual risks
- **Plain diff and non-UTF-8 names.** The plain fallback returns an error for any non-UTF-8 name in the repo, even an unrelated one, so that review is blocked.
- **Moved `.env`.** `mv .env notes.txt` of an untracked, ignored `.env` is undetectable. Only credential redaction guards it.
- **Predicate gaps.** `-diff` attributes, concurrent index changes in a shared checkout, and git older than 2.40 silently ignoring `GIT_ATTR_SOURCE` are not covered.
- **`operation_git.ts` git helpers.** Unpinned `gitResult` and `git` calls with a unit worktree as cwd exist there (the pre-existing issue you said is tracked separately).
- **Other blocking.** Non-predicate blockers on reviewers (path count over 2,000, driver refusal, timeouts) are unchanged, per your decision.

<result>**ACCEPT** for 559d69828df4294a71bd3e17189c5149bb92d4e5. I found no Blocker or Medium issues, and the prior Medium and both Lows are resolved. I only read the diff and code; nothing was built or run.

**(1) The check is correct.**
- `realRoot` comes from `realpath(root)`. The Linux `readlink("/proc/self/fd/N")` returns the kernel's resolved absolute path, so both sides of the comparison are realpaths. A root reached through a symlinked tmp dir or a bind mount still REDACTEDes.
- Both calls use the same mount namespace, so they agree.
- Every exit path closes the fd:
  - The `!isFile()` branch closes it.
  - The out-of-root branch closes it.
  - A throw from `readlinkSync` goes to the catch, which closes it.
  - The success path hands it to the caller. Both callers close it, one in `finally` and one on every branch.
- A deleted-then-unlinked regular file gives `"&lt;path&gt; (deleted)"`. That still starts with `realRoot/`, so it REDACTEDes (it does not produce a false "cannot"), and the open fd is harmless. A file unlinked and moved out of the root would show its new path. Non-file targets such as `pipe:[]` are already excluded by `isFile()`.
- Failure mode when `/proc` is missing:
  - **Low, `cli/src/core/specialist_inputs.ts:226-228`.** The catch maps `ENOENT` to "gone". If `/proc` is not mounted (some minimal containers or chroots), the failed readlink returns "gone" rather than "cannot", despite the "fails closed" comment.
  - Impact is limited: in `protectedContent`, "gone" only means "skip this source". In `carriedBy`, any string result returns `true`, which omits the file, so that path fails safe.
  - Fix, optional: make the readlink failure explicit, for example `try { resolved = readlinkSync(...) } catch { closeSync(fd); return "cannot"; }`. This also makes the "fails closed" claim literally true.

**(2) The new parameter is safe.** The only production callers (lines 248 and 276) use the default `fs.realpathSync`. Other uses are in the test, and `task_message_log.ts` has its own unrelated `openRegular`. The parameter widens nothing, because the descriptor check does not depend on it.

**(3) The non-Linux branch is documented honestly.** The code comment says "elsewhere the window stays narrowed". The FINDINGS A4a sentence says "on other platforms that window is narrowed, not closed".

**(4) The test is meaningful.**
- The stub returns an in-root path for `.../swapped` and the real path for everything else. The `inRoot` path is built from `realpathSync(d)`, so it REDACTEDes the directory check.
- The real `open` then follows the symlink out, and only the new descriptor check can produce "cannot". The author reports it RED without the check and green with it.
- It is skipped off Linux.
- **Nit:** the `outside` tmp dir is never removed. The existing test appears to leave its temp dirs too, so this is consistent.
- The FIFO assertion is now outside the swallowing catch.

<summary>Agent "Review #479 final redactor fix at 761f0b58" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>REQUEST_CHANGES at head 761f0b5803c4b56a2c83069988c63ae533a9ee84 (`origin/v1/v114-int-2` verified). It has one new starvation leak in the pattern loop. I read the code only and ran nothing, so the repro below and the test red/green calls are traced by hand.

### Blockers

- **Medium (low-to-medium impact, but it reopens the Low this delta claims to close).** `cli/src/core/egress.ts:1126`, `:1153`, `:1156`. `scannedTo` is declared outside the `for (const { name, regex } of DETERMINISTIC_PATTERNS)` loop, but that loop runs pattern by pattern, not in text order. A match in a later pattern that sits before the highest `scannedTo` of an earlier pattern is classified as a rescan. This holds even when it is nowhere near a window. `scannedTo = max(scannedTo, extended)` also advances on matches with no extension at all.
  - **Trigger:** put a plain `sk-ant-&lt;20+ chars&gt;` (or `sk-&lt;20+ chars&gt;`) at the END of the text. ANTHROPIC and OPENAI run first, so `scannedTo` jumps to roughly `n`. Every ghp_/AIza/hf_/xox/JWT match earlier in the text is then a "rescan" and shares the single `8*MAX_EXTENSION` = 8192-character quota.
  - **Example:** about 9 space-separated `ghp_…` tokens, each with a ~1000-character dotted tail, then a target `ghp_…​.TAIL` with a tail longer than what is left in the quota, then the dummy `sk-ant-…` at the end. Each earlier token is separated by spaces and not inside any window, so it is a fresh match that should have drawn on the main budget. The target's `chargeExtension` fails and the tail stays in clear. The `ghp_` plain form is still redacted.
  - **Why it matters:** it is not the documented limitation, because it needs no blob and the keys are whitespace-separated. It is a regression against the previous head, which had a budget of `n+1024` for every match. It is not a regression against main, which had no extensions at all.
  - **Fix:** make fresh versus rescan a per-pattern decision, with `scannedTo = 0` reset at the top of each pattern iteration. Windows are then disjoint within a pattern, and the main budget can be 7 times larger (`NUM_PATTERNS * (text.length + MAX_EXTENSION)`) or created per pattern. Alternatively, gather every pattern's matches and sort by start before the extension REDACTED. Add a test: a space-separated block of dotted ghp_ tokens, a target, and a trailing `sk-ant-…`.

### Suggestions

- `egress.ts:1155`. In the pattern loop, `budget.left &gt; 0` is the only guard on `extendDottedKey`. `chargeExtension` takes nothing when the extension does not fit, so once `left` is small but above 0, every later rescan keeps doing up to ~2 KB of scan plus `slice`/`normalize`/`test`. This is linear but with a large constant: a dot-joined chain of about 35-character JWTs or ghp_ tokens costs roughly 30-60 times `n`. The previous design capped total work by `min(MAX_EXTENSION, budget.left)`. `scanBase64Keys` has `rescanLeft = 16n`; the pattern loop has no equivalent. Fix: add the same `rescanLeft` work meter (`16 + extended - end` per rescan), or stop extending once a charge fails.
- `egress.ts:604` has the same shape on a smaller scale. A failed dotted probe, up to 1 KB, is charged only 16. Those probes are disjoint dot-separated segments, so it stays linear.
- `cli/test/redactor_encoded_dotted.test.ts` limitation test (`a real key inside a dense blob's window…`). Its assertion `!output.includes("Qx7Km2Vb9")` does not prove "its plain form is still redacted", which is the FINDINGS claim. The plain prefix `sk-Qx7Km2Vb9` is only 9 characters, so no `sk-` pattern matches it. If that assertion holds, it holds through some other detector, such as the whole-token entropy check on the blob.
- The `3*n+4096` bound in the extension-size test is an empirical constant. Per key, value length is at most `3 × (run + windows)`. Add the 2×8192 rescan quota plus the 1024 slack, and an adversarial input can exceed `3n+4096` by up to ~16 KB. This is bounded and harmless, but the test comment should not call it a guarantee.

### Verification by question

(a) **Monotone: confirmed for both loops.**
- **`scanBase64Keys`:** `runEnd` uses `scanKeyRun(text, at+3, false)`, which is equivalent to main's loop. The `end &gt; runEnd` guard exempts the original run from charging, so it is always judged and pushed. When the quota is spent, `extendedEnd = runEnd`. The only paths that `continue` are the longer candidates (`chargeExtension` false). The replacement set therefore only grows.
- **Pattern loop:** `ends = [end]` is always kept. The extension is pushed only when `extended &gt; end &amp;&amp; chargeExtension` succeeds. Nothing redacted at main can leak.

(b) **Linear cost: yes, with the caveat above.**
- **`scanBase64Keys` fresh work:** O(n), because fresh windows are disjoint (`at &gt;= scannedTo`) and runs are disjoint (`from = runEnd`).
- **`scanBase64Keys` rescan work:** capped at about `16n` plus one overshoot.
- **`isBase64KeyRun`:** about 3 candidates each of at most 2 KB, charged by the same meters.
- **Specific shapes:** `sk-%2F` repeated, accepted units joined by `-`, `sk-sk-…` and `sk-a sk-a …` are all O(n) with a constant of at most ~50. Long dotted chains are bounded to 1024 per match, and failed probes are partitioned by dot, so they stay O(n).
- **Pattern loop:** unbounded count of up-to-2 KB rescans, so about 30-60n (see the suggestion).

(c) **Starving a later real key.**
- **Key after whitespace in `scanBase64Keys`:** no shape starves it. It is always fresh, and the main budget `n+1024` can never be spent, since disjoint fresh charges sum to at most `n`.
- **Cheapest shape that works there:** the real key starts inside the first blob window within its last ~24 characters, is separated only by `-` or `.`, and about 9 accepted `sk-%2F0000000a-` units precede it. This is the documented no-separator limitation, so it is not a new leak. A hit costs about 200 characters of input.
- **The new leak:** the cross-pattern shape in the blocker. It works with whitespace separators, so it is not covered by the limitation.

(d) **Tests.**
- **Accepted-blob test:** red on the previous commit by my arithmetic. 200 units × ~1 KB charges exceed the `~94K` budget. The filler length is tuned to the constants, but it is a real red-first test.
- **Rejected-blob and ghp_ tests:** plausibly red on the previous commit, by the same budget arithmetic.
- **Loosened assertion (`total &gt; n/2`):** still meaningful, since it checks that fresh extensions are accepted.
- **Monotonicity corpus test and fixtures:** untouched and still protecting.
- **Gap:** no test covers cross-pattern ordering.

(e) **README accuracy.**
- **Worktree bullet:** correct. `--worktree` and `--worktree-branch` are opt-in (`cli/src/index.ts:349`, `:350`; `sidecar.ts:1262`), and `docs/WORKTREES.md` exists.
- **"By default" overclaims:** the only other hit is line 49, "Approval by Default: Unset permission mode is review", which is accurate. `docs/README.md` has no hits.

(f) **FINDINGS.md.** The diff is one added line and nothing removed, so no row is lost. The row's "its plain form is still redacted" is only true when the plain prefix alone matches a pattern. The row also says "the deterministic-pattern loop has its own budget with the same fresh/rescan split", which is accurate but does not disclose the cross-pattern flaw.

(g) **Picker tests.**
- **40x10 test:** now asserts the title, a model row and `╰`. `╰` is matched anywhere in the frame, not only on the picker panel (it could be any rounded border), so it is a weakish check on the bottom border. Acceptable.
- **Height test:** the literal `11` replaces a formula that restated the implementation. Better.

### Good
- The fresh/rescan split makes the main budget unspendable by construction, which is the right idea. It only needs to be applied per pattern.
- A key with both encoded and dotted extensions is charged once (`chargedTo`), and the original run is never charged.
- The picker tests now assert content instead of non-emptiness or a mirrored formula.
- The README claim now matches the CLI flags.

### Slop: Low
- The core design is sound and the changes are tightly scoped.
- The strongest negatives are the loosely justified limitation test and the 8×/16× magic constants, which are unnamed and undocumented.
- The pattern-loop `scannedTo` is a copied-without-adapting assumption of text-order processing.

Relevant paths: `/home/mike/nexus-wt/v114-int-2/cli/src/core/egress.ts` (lines 524-620, 1109-1176), `/home/mike/nexus-wt/v114-int-2/cli/test/redactor_encoded_dotted.test.ts`, `/home/mike/nexus-wt/v114-int-2/core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live_view.rs`.</result>

<result>ACCEPT `3b3171b1118379a6dfc9282fb8ce31a12e8f415a`, with two non-blocking Low findings on gitlink type changes. I read the diff and the surrounding code. I ran only git experiments in scratch repos under `/tmp/claude-1000/-home-mike-nexus-code/6473a60e-9fa2-4bd9-9d0c-35324295cf68/scratchpad/`, using git 2.43; I did not build or run the test suite.

### Blockers
No blockers found.

### Suggestions
- **Low, `cli/src/core/specialist_inputs.ts:494-502`, file → gitlink type change.** Real git raw output is `:100644 160000 &lt;sha&gt; &lt;sha&gt; T`, and the regex accepts it.
  - **Trigger:** a file path is replaced by a submodule. In my scratch repo, plain `git diff --ignore-submodules=all` against the base-tree index already printed the file deletion and a `new file mode 160000` hunk, because `=all` only suppresses entries that are gitlinks on both sides. `gitlinkHunks` then appends a second, identical `new file mode 160000` hunk for the same path.
  - **Impact:** there is no crash. The duplicate hunk uses the real-index sha. The first one uses the submodule HEAD that git reads from the agent-writable directory, so the two can disagree. That git read of the on-disk submodule HEAD already existed before this commit.
  - **Fix:** skip `!before &amp;&amp; after` entries in `gitlinkHunks` when the path is already in `tracked`, or filter type changes (status `T`) out of the append and handle them in one place.
- **Low, same function, gitlink → file type change.** Real raw output is `:160000 100644 … T`, and `diff-index --name-status` reports `T`, not `D`, so the path is not force-removed from the scratch index.
  - **Trigger:** a submodule is replaced by a regular file. `git diff --ignore-submodules=all` against the base-tree index printed nothing, and `--name-only` was empty. `gitlinkHunks` renders only `deleted file mode 160000`, so the new file's content is never shown to the reviewer.
  - **Impact:** the file content stays hidden. That blind spot existed before this commit and is only partly improved.
  - **Fix:** for `before &amp;&amp; !after` entries, either add the path to `stagedRemovals` so the file shows up as new content, or render it with `git diff --no-index -- /dev/null &lt;file&gt;`. Add a test for each type-change direction.

### Verification of the requested points
1. **Raw `-z` parsing.** The format is `:old new oldsha newsha S\0path\0`, and the regex matches it.
   - With `--no-renames` each record has exactly one path, so stepping by 2 is correct.
   - `--relative` yields relative paths. Status letters with scores cannot occur, but `\d*` tolerates them.
   - `[0-9a-f]+` covers 40 and 64 hex characters. SHA-256 is refused earlier anyway.
   - Unmerged entries have mode 000000 and are skipped, which is harmless because `snapshotSupport` and the conflict handling cover them elsewhere.
   - The hunk text matches git's own format for add, delete and modify: `new file mode`, `index 0000000..abc`, `@@ -0,0 +1 @@`, and `Subproject commit &lt;sha&gt;`.
2. **Trust boundary.** The new call goes through `git()` with `pin` and `GIT_SAFE`, using `ownEnv` and `--cached`, so it reads only the real index and never opens a submodule or the worktree. `--ignore-submodules=all` is still on both the `--name-only` call (line 549) and the rendering call (line 566).
3. **Protected paths.** `inputPathProtection` is applied per gitlink path, and protected ones are counted in `omitted`. `protectedChanged` is derived from `tracked`, which still excludes gitlinks. A protected gitlink therefore does not suppress new files. That is acceptable, since nothing was moved off disk, and it matches the old behaviour.
4. **`MAX_DIFF_PATHS`.** Gitlinks are not counted toward the cap. They are bounded by `LISTING_MAX_BUFFER` and by `boundedRedactedInput` truncation, so this is acceptable.
5. **`candidateDiff`.** `snapshotSupport` runs `ls-files -s -z` on the real index and refuses any mode-160000 entry, at stage 0 or unmerged, before any diff runs. A gitlink present only at the base and removed from the index is handled as a plain deletion, and the main builder has no `--ignore-submodules`, so it shows the pointer without opening the submodule. The main builder has no blind spot.
6. **Tests.** The integration test uses a real submodule and a real staged pointer change, and asserts the old and new shas, so it is meaningful. It also covers the mixed case. `-c protocol.file.allow=always` is harmless on git 2.34 on ubuntu-22.04 and needed on 2.38.1 or later, so it is not brittle. I did not check that `in/a.txt` exists in `repo()`. The author's red-without-the-append run covers the main behaviour. The unit test covers add, modify, delete, a non-gitlink entry and a protected path. Neither test covers type changes, which is the gap behind the two findings above.

<result>## REQUEST_CHANGES at ff662fa6ba4d28029669dbd38b20bf6a415eb57b

I verified that `origin/v1/v114-int-2` is ff662fa6. This was a read-only review: I ran no builds or tests, and the work-meter numbers below come from the constants in `cli/src/core/egress.ts` plus a small arithmetic sim of the meter, not from executing the suite.

### Blockers

- **`cli/src/core/egress.ts:~615-620` (`scanBase64Keys`) and `~1162-1168` (pattern loop): the fixed 8 KB rescan quota breaks monotonicity vs main 6e10ac9d. Severity Medium (a crafted-input leak; it breaks the stated invariant).**
  - Trigger (an ordinary shape, not the documented limitation):
    - Dot-joined key material, about 8 KB long, with a real key every ~1 KB: `ghp_&lt;36&gt;` + `.Zk4Pq8R1` filler segments + `.ghp_&lt;36&gt;` + …. Each later key starts inside the previous key's window and ends near its edge.
    - Padding elsewhere in the text, or no padding at all: the 8 KB main shape alone already gives main a ~9 KB budget.
    - A final target key (`ghp_…` + ~100 characters of `.Mn5Qr7Vw0…` tail) sits inside the last window.
  - Why it leaks now: each rescan charges about 1.04 KB (its end plus 1024, minus the previous window end), so about 7 rescans spend `RESCAN_QUOTA_FACTOR * MAX_EXTENSION` = 8192. The target's extension is then refused. No fresh key follows to carry the tail, so the tail stays in clear.
  - Why main kept it: main charged each extension in full against `text.length + 1024` (about 9 KB for this text, 100 KB+ with padding).
  - How it compares to the documented limits: the FINDINGS row and code comment say "several hundred keys" or "a dot-joined chain of ~1000 keys", but the real threshold is about 8 keys of ~1 KB spacing.
  - Fix:
    - Keep the cheap path: charge only the characters past what was already scanned, against the 8 KB quota.
    - On failure, fall back to main's rule. Charge the full `to - end` against a per-family `{ left: text.length + MAX_EXTENSION }` budget. That budget is at least main's, so anything main extended is still extended.
    - The value size stays bounded by N + 1 KB, as at main.
  - Test: a sparse ~8 KB dotted chain with 1 KB key spacing and a long-tailed key last, plus 100 KB of padding. It should be red at this head.
  - `RESCAN_QUOTA_FACTOR` is a constant, not text-scaled. Scaling it with text length would not work: 14-character overlapping units would then keep about 73n of values.

- **`cli/src/sidecar.ts:651-653`: Entire Low 01M76HYVT92E is real. It was introduced by #472, not present at main. Severity Low (confidence 0.9, traced in code).**
  - What the defect is: `let saved = true; try { withDurableSync(() =&gt; this.saveSession(label, turn)) }`. `saveSession` returns silently when `messages.length &lt;= 1` (`sidecar.ts:924`).
  - Trigger:
    - A first `--worktree` turn fails before the prompt is appended. `agent.runTurn` (`agent.ts:5971-6010`) routes to `this.run`, which creates the worktree. A sandbox or worktree setup error there throws before the user message is added.
    - `failedTurnFromError` produces a turn, and the conversation is still system-prompt only.
    - The skipped write counts as saved, so `turn.result` carries `saved: true`.
  - Impact: `live_state.rs:967` does `session_saved |= saved`, so the exit breadcrumb prints "Nexus session X saved. Resume with: nexus --resume X" for a session that was never written. The `saved` flag and breadcrumb are new in #472: the flag is the `d199dfae` hunk, and main has no `session_saved`.
  - Fix:
    - Make `saveSession` return `boolean` (`false` at the `&lt;= 1` early return, `true` after `manager.saveSession`).
    - Use `saved = withDurableSync(() =&gt; this.saveSession(label, turn))` and keep the existing catch setting `false`. The early-return branch is the only path that needs the new return value.
    - Other callers can ignore the return.
  - Test: a sidecar test where `runTurn` throws before any message is added. Assert the `turn.result` event has `saved === false`. A second case with a normal turn asserts `true`.

### Suggestions

- **`cli/test/redactor_encoded_dotted.test.ts:~249-272` (the time-bound test): flakiness risk on loaded CI.**
  - Context:
    - It is a wall-clock `&lt; 5000` ms per input, and the input list was not changed by this diff.
    - The 900 KB `"sk-%2FAAAAAAA0-".repeat(60000)` blob is the heaviest input. By the author's own numbers it already takes several seconds.
    - Worst-case work is now about 16·N per family. That is 1 for `scanBase64Keys`, plus up to 7 dotted-pattern families when the text matches them, so roughly 100N+ character steps. Main was about N.
  - A runner 2x slower than the author's machine fails it. The new tests use the same 5 s bound on 300–400 KB inputs.
  - Fix:
    - Shrink the blobs: 900 KB to about 60–100 KB, and the new tests' `40000/20000` repeats to about 5000.
    - Raise the bound to a generous 20–30 s, or assert scaling instead. At 100 KB the work is still 5000 times the quadratic gap, so the bound still catches a quadratic regression.
    - Better: export a scanned-character counter and assert `&lt;= 130 * text.length`.

- **`cli/src/core/egress.ts:~609,1165` (`RESCAN_WORK_FACTOR` meter): the work meter charges `16 + extended - runEnd` (full window), not past-scanned.**
  - A dense chain of keys with ~41-character spacing burns about 1040 per rescan against a 656/key budget. It exhausts after about 63% of the keys (about key 65 of 100).
  - Per my sim the 40/60/100 chain tests still REDACTED. After exhaustion, keys past the last window are "fresh" and carry the tail.
  - The leak window is a last key plus tail straddling the last window edge. It exists for a narrow band of chain lengths, about 65–70 dense keys.
  - Fix: reuse the earlier scan end (resume dotted scanning from `scannedTo` when the run is contiguous), then meter new characters only.
  - Reword the FINDINGS row and the code comment to match reality.

- **`cli/src/core/egress.ts:~609,1166`: the literal `16` in `rescanLeft -= 16 + …` appears twice and is unnamed.** The same fresh/rescan machinery is duplicated across the two loops. Extract a small `ExtensionMeter` helper to avoid divergence like the one in the first blocker.

- **The `6n+4096` bound (test ~line 165): meaningful but empirical.** The author's comment calls it "empirical, not guarantees" (about 5n observed). It would catch a 10x or larger regression, but it is not the proof of the value bound. It is also weaker than the previous 3n, a loosening that makes the first blocker invisible. Pair it with the sparse-chain test above.

### Checks (a)–(f), concretely

- **(a) Monotone vs 6e10ac9d.**
  - Both loops always emit the original run or match. `runEnd` and `ends=[end]` are never charged, and `chargeExtension` only gates `end &gt; runEnd`.
  - The replacement set only grows except where extensions main accepted are now refused: the first blocker.
- **(b) Linear work.** Fresh windows are disjoint. Rescans are metered to 16N + one overshoot of about 2 KB per family. Fresh rejected dotted scans are bounded by the next dot. Total is about 130N character-steps. No quadratic path found.
- **(c) Starving a later key.** Cheapest shape: about 8 KB of ~1 KB-spaced dotted keys, then a long-tailed key. This leaks and is new relative to both main and the documented limitation (the first blocker). Cross-pattern starvation is gone: one pattern's keys no longer spend another's budget, as separate per-pattern budgets confirm. Dense chains: see the work-meter suggestion above.
- **(d) Tests.**
  - The new tests are plausibly red-first, but I did not run them. I could only verify by reading that they exercise the right budget paths.
  - The monotone corpus fixtures (`cli/test/fixtures/redaction_monotone_corpus.js`) are untouched: no diff to that fixture directory.
- **(f) FINDINGS.** Exactly one row was added and no other row lost (`+1/-0`). The row's "several hundred keys" limitation text is not truthful (first blocker, work-meter suggestion).
- **README, `live_view.rs`:** the opt-in worktree wording is accurate. The model-picker tests now assert real content (`assert_shows`, the `╰` border, the 11-row panel).

### Good

- The fix finally isolates budgets per pattern and fresh-vs-rescan, and the original match is never dropped.
- Charging only the characters past what was already scanned is the right primitive.
- The code comments state the invariant honestly, including "empirical, not guarantees".
- The added tests cover cross-pattern, chain, and accepted-vs-rejected blob shapes.

### Slop rating: Medium

- Two near-copy budget state machines with a magic `16` and a quota constant that is neither principled nor text-scaled.
- Five iterations of the same fix left a documented limitation that understates the real one (about 8 keys, not several hundred), plus a wall-clock test that sits close to its bound.

<result>**ACCEPT** at head `ff37378258dfd4918ddcea52ccb17b28586699db`. I read the diff only and did not run the tests, so the red/green claims in item 3 rest on the author's report.

I fetched origin; the head matches the expected SHA. The delta from `3b3171b1` touches only `cli/src/core/specialist_inputs.ts` (+11/−4) and `cli/test/specialist_inputs_domain.test.ts` (+36).

1. **`gitlinkHunks` skip for `T`:** `specialist_inputs.ts:~494` skips a `T` entry whose old mode is not `160000`, which is the file-replaced-by-submodule case. The regex captures the status letter as group 5, and the destructuring now reads `status` from it. A submodule replaced by a file (old mode `160000`) still gets its `deleted file mode 160000` hunk. Pure add (`A`), pure delete (`D`) and a pointer modification (`M`) are unaffected.

2. **Single `--raw` read:**
   - The command is `diff-index --cached --raw -z --relative --no-renames &lt;commit&gt;`, run once with `ownIndex`, and it goes through `git`, which applies `pin` and `GIT_SAFE`.
   - `--raw -z` output has the same meta-NUL-path-NUL pairing as the old `--name-status -z` output, and `--no-renames` rules out two-path entries. The `i += 2` loop and the trailing empty field are handled correctly.
   - A `D` entry gives the same removal list as before, so ordinary staged removals are preserved. The only addition is `T` with old mode `160000`.
   - `update-index --force-remove -z --stdin` input is still `join("\0") + "\0"`.
   - `gitlinkHunks` reuses the same `stagedRaw`, so the two reads can no longer disagree. The `T`/`160000` removal and its `deleted file mode 160000` hunk are applied once each, and git's own diff shows only the new file's content.
   - A `T` from a regular file or symlink with no gitlink on either side is not removed, which is correct.

3. **Tests:** both tests assert the specific outcome rather than only the absence of an error.
   - File replaced by submodule: exactly one `new file mode 160000` hunk plus a `Subproject commit` line. This fails if the new skip is removed, because the pointer would then be emitted twice.
   - Submodule replaced by file: a `deleted file mode 160000` hunk with `-Subproject commit`, plus `+now a plain file`. This fails if the `T`/`160000` removal is dropped, because the file would then be hidden behind the gitlink.
   - The assertion that each test fails with its change disabled is the author's claim, which I did not re-run.

4. **Nothing else changed, trust boundary intact:**
   - The only new git call is the merged `--raw` read, which is index-only and goes through `pin` and `GIT_SAFE`. The extra `--raw` call in the old gitlink step is gone.
   - `--ignore-submodules=all` is still on both diff calls, at lines 556 and 573.
   - No submodule directory is opened.

**Suggestion (Low, no change required):** the file-to-submodule case depends on git's diff against the private index, run with `--ignore-submodules=all`, printing the new gitlink. The new test asserts this, so it is covered, but confirm it ran green on your side (no build or run on mine).</result>

**Head:** `0c0c5cb1`, pushed to `v1/v114-int-2` (it was `ff662fa6`). Nothing was approved, merged or dispatched.

**Egress change**
- All the budget machinery is deleted from `cli/src/core/egress.ts`: `ExtensionBudget`, `chargeExtension`, `rescanLeft`, `scannedTo`, `chargedTo` and the two `RESCAN_*` factors.
- Every candidate key now extends on its own, capped at `MAX_EXTENSION` (1024) characters.
- Each family has a per-call counter `MAX_EXTENSION_ATTEMPTS_PER_FAMILY` (1024): the `sk-`/`sk-ant-` family, and each `DETERMINISTIC_PATTERNS` entry. With none left, only the original match is emitted.
- The original match is always emitted.
- An attempt is only spent when a `.` or an encoded character directly follows the candidate, so ordinary text does not use them up.
- The honest-limit sentence is in the code comment and in the FINDINGS row for `01M76CJKRA0T`. I also reworded the old multi-limitation text.

**Sidecar fix (`01M76HYVT92E`)**
- `saveSession` now returns a boolean: false at the early return, true after the session is written.
- `turn.result.saved` takes that value, and the catch still sets it to false.
- A new test in `cli/test/sidecar.test.ts` covers a normal turn (true) and a turn that throws before any message is added (false, nothing written).

**Tests**
- I added tests for the sparse 1 KB-spacing chain, the cross-pattern case with a trailing `sk-ant-` key, the 200-blob accepted case, the dense three-prefix chain, and the attempt cap. The cap tests assert the plain form is redacted with a 20 s time bound, plus a value-size bound.
- I deleted the tests that only pinned the removed budget machinery, shrank the heavy inputs, and loosened the time bound to 20 s.
- The monotonicity corpus test is green.
- Verification: `tsc` is clean. All redact*, egress* and persistence suites plus `sidecar_diagnostic_redaction` REDACTED (112 of 112).
- `sidecar.test.ts` has 10 failures, all "Rust core RPC daemon disconnected", which is the unbuilt Rust core on this host. I did not diff them against `origin/main`. My new test REDACTEDes.

**Pre-push review**
- Monotonicity against `origin/main` holds.
- Extension work is constant, from the constants.
- The reviewer showed my "constant memory" claim was wrong: extension values are at most about 3 times the text, because each repeats its original run. I corrected the code comment, the FINDINGS row and the test bound.
- The reviewer found that a cheap blob such as `"sk-. "` repeated 1025 times (about 5 KB) spends the `sk-` family's attempts without being a real key. That is a gap against my "key-like" wording, so I redefined "candidate" in the comment and the FINDINGS row instead of tightening the code. Tightening it would need validating each candidate before spending an attempt, which brings back accounting. The practical limit for the `sk-` family is therefore lower than a real-key count. If you want it fixed in code, that is one more round.

<output-file>/tmp/claude-1000/-home-mike-nexus-code/6473a60e-9fa2-4bd9-9d0c-35324295cf68/tasks/a620a1677e92584b1.output</output-file>

<summary>Agent "Review #479 at 0c0c5cb1" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>REQUEST_CHANGES at head `0c0c5cb14127ec14fbaaa2994ce8a5aa4e123dd5` (branch `v1/v114-int-2`).

The redaction logic is monotone and the sidecar fix is correct. I am requesting changes because the "constant" work and value bound is large and unproven, and its guard test cannot fail. This is a read-only review: I did not build or run anything, so the cost figures below are estimates from the code.

## Findings

**1. Medium (performance/DoS), `cli/src/core/egress.ts:492-501, 1114-1150`**
The bound is constant, but the constant is large. At zero attempts only the original is emitted, so this is not a leak, only a cost.
- **Per-extension cost:** each attempt scans up to 1024 chars for the encoded run and up to another 1024 for the dotted run, plus a slice and normalize per segment.
- **Work across families:** 8 families x 1024 attempts x ~2-3 KB is about 25 MB of character work. That alone is tens of milliseconds.
- **Values:** the `sk-` family can add about 1024 x 2 KB, and each of the 7 patterns about 1024 x 1 KB, so roughly 9-10 MB of value characters beyond 3x the text.
- **Downstream cost:** every value goes through `replaceValues` and `buildAutomaton` (`egress.ts:730-760`). That means a `Map&lt;number,number&gt;` trie with one entry per character and 7 Int32Arrays sized to the value total. With more than 16 values it takes the Aho-Corasick path.
- **Trigger:** about 40 KB of dense dotted keys per family (`ghp_&lt;36&gt;.Zk4Pq8x001.Zk4Pq8x002...`, one such chain per family) feeds all 8 families. That is a text of about 300-400 KB. It would give about 8-10M trie nodes, which is seconds and hundreds of MB. A 3 MB text could also push the Map past V8's roughly 16.7M-entry limit and throw a RangeError inside redaction.
- **Test evidence:** the author halved the adversarial inputs and raised the bound from 5 s to 20 s, which suggests the times were not small. A 100 KB text should cost about 1 s; 1 MB could cost several seconds.
- **Origin/main comparison:** origin/main has no extension at all, so this cost is new.
- **Fix:** lower `MAX_EXTENSION_ATTEMPTS_PER_FAMILY` (for example 128-256) and/or `MAX_EXTENSION` for dotted extension, so the constant is about 1-2 MB. The cost is a smaller starvation threshold. Alternatively keep one per-call counter of extended characters (`takeAttempt` charging the extension length), which is a single number and not the deleted rescan machinery.

**2. Medium (test), `cli/test/redactor_encoded_dotted.test.ts` (`EXTENSION_VALUE_ALLOWANCE = 32 * 1024 * 1024`, and the 20 s bounds)**
The value-size bound is not meaningful as a guard.
- 32 MB is above the real theoretical maximum of about 10 MB, so `total &lt;= 3*len + 32 MB` cannot fail for any input these tests build.
- The 20 s wall-clock bound would REDACTED a 4x regression.
- **Fix:** derive the allowance from the constants (for example `families * ATTEMPTS * (2*MAX_EXTENSION + run)`, exported or duplicated) and set it near 2x the measured value. For time, assert a work proxy such as the count of values or total value characters, not wall clock.

**3. Low (docs overclaim), `docs/v1.1.4/FINDINGS.md` (new row) and `cli/src/core/egress.ts:495`**
- FINDINGS says "extension values add at most ~3x the text". The code comment correctly says "plus that constant", and that constant is about 10 MB. State the constant explicitly.
- The comment says "key-like candidates", but a candidate is any `sk-` run followed by "." or an encoded character. That includes the 4-byte `sk-.`, which is not key-like.
- Cheapest starvation shape: `sk-.` x1025 is about 4 KB with no space needed, against the 5 KB the brief quoted. Prefixing it before a real `sk-&lt;body&gt;%2F…` or dotted key leaves only the plain body redacted and the dotted tail in clear.
- It is acceptable as a documented limitation. The plain key is always emitted, nothing is worse than origin/main (which never extended), and `sk-.` x1025 is not realistic input.
- The honest wording is: "a candidate is any `sk-` run, or pattern match, directly followed by '.' (or an encoded char for `sk-`), key-like or not." Either tighten the code to count only runs that REDACTED a minimal body check (at least 20 body characters, or one separator) or reword.

**4. Low (test discrimination)**
- By my reading, the sparse-chain test (12 keys, 1 KB apart) and the nine-long-tails test probably also REDACTED on ff662fa6. Its main budget was `text.length + 1024`, about 105 KB and 11 KB respectively, and the windows there are disjoint. I did not run them.
- The 40/100-key dense-chain tests (`ghp_`, `sk-ant-`, `sk-`, and the three-prefix variant) plausibly fail on ff662fa6. 100 rescans of about 1 KB exceed `rescanLeft = 16*text.length` (about 65 KB for a 4 KB chain), so later keys lose their extension.
- The cross-pattern, accepted-blob and attempt-cap tests are plausibly discriminating for earlier heads' budget sharing. The attempt-cap test pins the new design.
- Fix: either accept as is, or make the sparse test deterministic against the old logic. That needs a text short enough that `text.length + 1024` is smaller than the summed extensions.

**5. Low (slop, scope), `core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live_view.rs` tests and `README.md:50`**
- The delta vs 00b9cd4a includes a README worktree opt-in sentence and a model-picker test rewrite from bfb87a52. Neither is mentioned in the review brief.
- The rewrite drops the `(rows+5).clamp(8,24)` relationship, so the test now pins only the constant 11, not the clamp.
- It does strengthen the 40x10 test, which now asserts content and the bottom border.
- Fix: mention both in the PR description, or restore the clamp assertion.

## Checks

1. **Monotone vs b696c3d2: holds.**
   - The base `scanBase64Keys` judged only the original run.
   - The head always judges `runEnd`. `encodedEnd` and `extendedEnd` are added only when `takeAttempt` succeeds.
   - With attempts at zero, `extend` is false and the set is `{runEnd}`, the same as the base.
   - The pattern path always pushes `ends=[end]` first, and the extension is a second push.
   - `from = Math.max(runEnd, at+1)` is unchanged. The replacement set only grows.
2. **Work and memory bound:** see finding 1.
3. **Starvation:** see finding 3.
4. **Test protection:**
   - The corpus and monotone fixtures are untouched. Only `sk-`/pattern tests pinning the budget were replaced.
   - There are no leftover references to `ExtensionBudget`, `chargeExtension`, `rescanLeft`, `scannedTo`, `chargedTo` or `RESCAN_` (`rg` over the repo returned none).
   - No dead constants remain. `MAX_EXTENSION`, `MAX_EXTENSION_ATTEMPTS_PER_FAMILY` and `takeAttempt` are all used.
5. **Sidecar:**
   - Fix is correct. `saveSession` returns false only at the `messages.length &lt;= 1` early return and true after writing. The turn path takes `saved = withDurableSync(...)`, and the catch gives false.
   - The other callers are `setProgressListener` callbacks and the `saveSession: (title) =&gt;` callback at `sidecar.ts:837`. They ignore the return value, and `withDurableSync&lt;T&gt;` is generic, so the types hold.
   - The Rust consumer reads `saved` with a default of true (`protocol.rs:157`), so nothing changes for it.
   - The new test is meaningful: normal turn gives true, and a throwing `runTurn` gives false with no session written. It is synthetic, though, because the real `runTurn` appends the prompt before failing, so in practice that case saves true.
   - The 10 `sidecar.test.ts` failures "Rust core RPC daemon disconnected" come from `cli/src/tools.ts:827`, the unbuilt Rust core. That matches the host-env pattern in memory and is plausibly pre-existing, but I did not run them at base.
6. **Comments and FINDINGS:**
   - Comments are trimmed. The `scanBase64Keys` doc block still carries a long first paragraph that predates this delta.
   - The FINDINGS diff is one added row and no rows lost. The row is accurate except for the overclaims in finding 3. It is a very long cell and could be split.

## Security
No new vulnerability. Redaction cannot drop an original match, so the change is a cost issue (finding 1) and not a data-exposure regression. The remaining exposure is the documented tail-in-clear case after more than 1024 candidates, which is no worse than origin/main.

## Slop: Low-to-Medium
- The two strongest reasons are the vacuous 32 MB allowance with a 20 s wall-clock guard (finding 2), and the docs and comments overclaiming the bound and redefining "candidate" (finding 3).
- The deletion of the budget machinery is a good simplification. The `ExtensionAttempts` interface with `takeAttempt`/`newExtensionAttempts` is a small, clean replacement.

## Good
- Deleting five iterations of accounting for one counter per family is the right shape, and the original-always-emitted invariant is stated at the point of use.
- The `scanBase64Keys` doc comment states the monotone invariant clearly.
- The `saveSession` boolean fix is minimal, and the new sidecar test checks both the true and false paths and the empty session list.</result>

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**REQUEST_CHANGES** at head `63c6450d2237b5180062e2a8aac6e1bb2a49e43d`, for one Low docs defect. Code and tests are acceptable. Nothing was built or run, so the cost figures below are estimates from the code.

## Findings

**1. Low (docs, false statement), `docs/v1.1.4/FINDINGS.md:119`**
- The 01M76CJKRA0T row says the tests assert the bounds "with a scanned-character counter instead of wall-clock".
- That counter was removed in 63c6450d. `redactor_encoded_dotted.test.ts` now uses `assertBounded`, a value-size bound plus a 20 s wall clock (lines 162-167), and no counter exists.
- Fix: reword to "asserts the values total at most 3n + 4096 and a generous 20 s wall clock on inputs up to 200 KB".

**2. Low (unstated total), `cli/src/core/egress.ts:492-503` comment and FINDINGS**
- The bound is documented per family ("each family's values add at most ~3n"). The whole-call total is not stated.
- A text can be one dot-joined chain with a candidate of every prefix: sk-, sk-ant-, ghp_, AIza, hf_, xox, eyJ. In that case all 8 families extend over the same characters.
- The `sk-` family is at most about 3n, and each of the 7 patterns at most about 2n (original plus extension). The whole call is therefore at most about 15-17n value characters, not 3n.
- Add one sentence saying so.
- Optionally add a 1 MB dense mixed-prefix chain test that asserts the total against the real bound.

## Verification

1. **Monotone: holds.**
   - The `sk-` candidate list is built by the same loop as the base, with the same `from = Math.max(runEnd, at+1)`. The `\w` boundary skip is unchanged and runs of one family are disjoint.
   - The original `runEnd` is always in the judged set, and `extensionLimit` is clamped by `Math.max(end, …)`.
   - A next candidate starting at `runEnd+1` (`sk-.sk-`) gives an empty window and no extension. `scanKeyRun` never crosses the limit.
   - Pattern family: candidates are pushed only after the skip and trim steps, so a rejected or reference-like match still bounds the previous window.
   - Regex matches do not overlap, so the next group start is at least the previous original end. `ends=[end]` is pushed first and the extension is an extra push.
   - An empty window yields no extension. The replacement set only grows versus origin/main.
   - One behaviour change versus 0c0c5cb1: a key with a valid inner candidate in its tail is now emitted as two values and no longer as one longer one. That is covered by the new "tail contains sk-" test.
2. **Work and memory bound:**
   - Per family, work is linear. Windows are disjoint, and `scanKeyRun`, `extendDottedKey` and `isBase64KeyRun` (3 judged ends, each linear) are bounded by the candidate's own region.
   - Whole call: at most about 8n of scanning, and about 15-17n value characters in the worst crafted chain (finding 2).
   - At 1 MB of such a chain, the trie has roughly 8-10M nodes. That is a multi-second build with several hundred MB to about 1 GB, mostly the `Map&lt;number,number&gt;` plus 7 Int32Arrays of the summed value length.
   - The V8 Map limit is about 16.7M entries. A RangeError is not expected at 1 MB, but it becomes possible somewhere around 2 MB or more of that shape.
   - This needs a deliberately crafted all-prefix dot-joined chain, and it is roughly a 4-5x amplification over origin/main's ≤ about 3-4n of values. I consider it acceptable and no longer a blocker: it is linear and cannot be tuned further by repeating a cheap pattern.
3. **Starvation:**
   - Cheap blobs of any length (`sk-%2F-`, `sk-. `), chains and cross-family text cannot starve a later key. Windows are bounded by that family's next candidate start, which cannot lie before the real key.
   - The only shape that cuts a key's tail is one where the real key's own tail contains another same-family candidate start that is invalid. For `sk-` that needs a `-`, `.`, `+` or `/` followed by `sk-` inside the secret. That is the documented limit, it is negligible for random keys, and the original match is still emitted.
4. **Tests:**
   - The cheap-blob test at N=200 would REDACTED on 0c0c5cb1, since 200 is under 1024 attempts. N=2000 and N=20000 would fail there. The attempts were exhausted, and the plain `sk-Qx7Km2Vb9` has under 20 characters of body, so the real key's pieces would stay in clear.
   - They REDACTED here.
   - The value-size assertion can now fail. The "original match always redacted" test (50 x `sk-&lt;38&gt;.`) would exceed 3n + 4096 under overlapping windows, since each pattern extension would run through the chain.
   - The `sk-%2F-` blob tests give about 0 value characters, so the assertion is vacuous for them.
   - The monotone corpus test and fixtures are untouched.
   - A 20 s wall clock on inputs of 200 KB or less is robust. It still fails a quadratic regression and does not flake.
5. **Sidecar:** `saved` is unchanged from the version I verified at 0c0c5cb1 and is still correct for every caller.
6. **Leftovers:** `rg` for `ExtensionBudget|chargeExtension|rescanLeft|scannedTo|chargedTo|RESCAN_|ExtensionAttempts|takeAttempt|MAX_EXTENSION_ATTEMPTS|scanWork` found nothing. The FINDINGS row for 01M76CJKRA0T was replaced and no rows were lost, but it carries the false counter claim (finding 1).

## Slop: Low
- The two remaining issues are the stale "scanned-character counter" sentence in FINDINGS and the missing whole-call total.
- The collect-then-window design is smaller and simpler than any earlier iteration, and the comments are tight.

## Good
- Disjoint windows give a structural bound with no state to starve.
- The tests no longer depend on a test-only counter.
- Every previously failing shape now has a regression test.

<result>**ACCEPT** at head `3d1e68945721118315d61190d6bcc3bdda9e07e9`, no blockers. This was a read-only check (git and file reads); I did not run the tests.

**Delta.** `git diff 2ffd1bad..origin/v1/v114-int-2` touches one file, `cli/test/redactor_encoded_dotted.test.ts` (+31/-3). No production file changed.

**What changed.**
- The `assertBounded` comment now says the wall clock only catches a severe blowup and points to `assertLinear`. That removes the Entire Low 01M76NF4R1YW mismatch.
- `bestTime` takes the minimum of three runs. `assertLinear` builds the same shape at 50 KB and 200 KB and asserts `tLarge &lt;= 10 * tSmall + 250`.

**Shapes are at the claimed sizes.**
- The unit lengths are right: `sk-%2F-` is 7, `sk-. ` is 5, and `unitKey` is 15.
- The ghp_ key is 4 + 36 characters. With `.Zk4Pq8Rt01` the dotted-chain unit is 52 including the join dot, so `/52` is exact.
- The space-separated unit is also 52 characters, not the 53 the code divides by, which is about 2% off and harmless.

**Is the bound flaky?** No.
- `tSmall` is not pathologically small. 50 KB means 7-10K candidates through about 15 patterns, so it is milliseconds to tens of milliseconds. Best-of-three removes JIT and GC outliers, and the large size is timed second.
- A linear regression expects about 4x, well inside 10x + 250 ms.

**Detection limits (Low, optional).**
- A true quadratic fails only when `tSmall` exceeds about 42 ms, since 16t &gt; 10t + 250. In practice an unbounded window gives seconds at 50 KB and fails clearly. A mildly superlinear regression with a small `tSmall` can REDACTED because of the 250 ms slack.
- A per-candidate 1024-character window (the 0c0c5cb1 style) is linear in n with a large constant, so `assertLinear` cannot see it. The `assertBounded` value-size bound is what covers that, and `assertLinear` does not call it. Adding `valueChars` to the same shapes, or a ratio on the dense dotted ghp_ chain only, would close this.

**Unit.**
- `valueChars` is `classifyText`, which is where the extension scans happen, so it is the right unit for the claim "extension work is linear".
- It excludes `replaceValues` and `buildAutomaton`, where the many-distinct-values cost sits. The repeated identical keys in the chain shapes also dedupe to very few values, so those shapes barely exercise the automaton.
- If you want the automaton covered, time `redactDiagnosticForOutput` for the accepted-blob shape, which has distinct `unitKey` values.</result>

<summary>Agent "Pre-push review of #434 object-store fix" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**ACCEPT** — HEAD `8668cf28fa33121c652b910354bfeb6390d05454`. I found no blockers and no findings that need a change before push.

**1. Correctness.** I reproduced it in a scratch repo with a linked worktree.
- The setup was: blobs for a root and a subdirectory `.gitattributes` written only to an alternate store, `add -A`, `write-tree` and `commit-tree -p HEAD`.
- `printf '&lt;commit&gt;\n^HEAD\n' | git pack-objects --revs --quiet &lt;objects&gt;/pack/pack` exited 0 without `--local`, so the alternate objects went into the pack.
- After unsetting `GIT_ALTERNATE_OBJECT_DIRECTORIES` and deleting the alternate, `git fsck --connectivity-only` returned 0 and `cat-file -p &lt;commit&gt;:.gitattributes` worked.
- `rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-path objects` in the linked worktree returns the common `r/.git/objects`. That is the same directory an ordinary `git add` or commit writes to, so there is no unusual placement or permission issue.
- `HEAD` resolves in the unit worktree, the same one `commit-tree -p HEAD` used.
- Failure handling is fail-closed: `execFileSync` throws on a non-zero exit, so the snapshot throws, which matches the method's documented contract. The trimmed stdout (the pack hash) is ignored.

**2. Trust boundary.** Both new calls go through `run`, so they keep `pin`, `GIT_SAFE` and `safeGitEnv`. `ownIndex: true` is REDACTEDed, so the scratch index is not used. `pack-objects` only reads objects. It runs no hooks, filters, attribute drivers or textconv, and I did not find an unpinned call. `gc.auto=0` is set, so no automatic gc runs.

**3. Edge cases.**
- With `attrs` undefined, no alternate is in use and no pack is written, which is correct.
- When the stripped blob differs from the real one, git writes its own copy. The pack then holds the same commit, tree and blob objects the repository already has, which is harmless.
- A `.gitattributes` in a subdirectory is covered. My scratch test included `sub/.gitattributes`, which was also alternate-only, and fsck REDACTEDed.
- A tree identical to the attribute tree, for example a repo containing only `.gitattributes`, is also covered, because `--revs` walks every object the commit reaches.
- `^HEAD` limits the pack to objects new since HEAD, so the cost is proportional to the diff.
- Pack accumulation:
  - A repeated snapshot with identical content gives the same pack name and is idempotent (I re-ran it).
  - Different content leaves one small pack per snapshot, and only when `.gitattributes` is present. That is acceptable, and `git gc` will consolidate them later. You could mention it in a comment, but it is not required.
- Pack-objects failure paths: `pack-objects` writes a temporary file and removes it on error. It does not write a final pack when it fails.

**4. Same class of bug elsewhere.** No other path is affected.
- `rg` shows `attributesTree` has exactly two callers: `snapshotCandidate` and `candidateDiff` (`specialist_inputs.ts:394`).
- `candidateDiff` produces no commit. Its only writes are `read-tree`, `add --intent-to-add` (empty blob) and diff text consumed in-process, all in a scratch index.
- Nothing from `candidateDiff` outlives the scratch directory.
- `snapshotSupport` runs before `attrs` is set.

**5. Test.** The test is meaningful.
- It reproduces the trigger: a new unstaged `.gitattributes` containing `* text=auto`.
- It reads `HEAD:.gitattributes` and the edited file from the debugger worktree.
- It runs `fsck --connectivity-only`, and it checks the checked-out file on disk.
- You reported it red before the fix and green after. I did not run it, as instructed.

**Minor, non-blocking.** `pack-objects` is not covered for a snapshot with no new objects. Even then it behaves correctly, because an empty `--revs` set produces no pack.</result>
