<result>D-02 and D-08 are fixed and pushed to `v1/v114-tui-scroll-stall` at head `737a5e6267f48a9a88dc3654ef40b2d012df8ec4`. I did not open a PR, approve or merge anything.

## Fixes

**D-02, scrolled-back view drifted while an answer streamed.** The scroll anchor counted rows from the end of its entry, so one growing streaming answer slid the window. It is now (entry, line in the entry, row within the line), counted from the start. It is clamped if the entry, line or width changed. The changes are in `core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live_view.rs` and `live_state.rs`.

**D-08, stalled provider stream was never reported.** The stream watchdog in `cli/src/client.ts` now reports 30 s without a chunk as a new protocol event, `stream.stall`. The status line then reads `thinking · 0:45 · no data from the provider for 44 s · step 1 · esc cancels`. The count keeps running on the TUI's clock until data arrives, a tool starts, the phase changes or the turn ends.
- **Interval:** the 30 s default can be changed with `LOCAL_LLM_STALL_NOTICE_MS`; 0 turns it off.
- **Idle timeout:** the existing 120 s idle timeout is unchanged.
- **Wiring:** the event goes through `agent.ts` and `sidecar.ts`; `protocol.ts` defines it and `protocol.rs` parses it.

## Tests, red first

Each test was run and seen to fail before its fix, then REDACTEDes after. All 315 `nexus-tui` tests REDACTED, as do the new TS tests and `client_robustness`, and `tsc` is clean.
- **D-02:** `live_view::diff_tests::a_scrolled_back_view_keeps_its_top_row_while_one_answer_streams_below_it` failed with "the top row moved after 64 rows". It covers one streaming entry, wrapped lines, deltas arriving in pieces, and resizes to 70, 40 and 100 columns.
- **D-02:** `a_scrolled_back_view_survives_tiny_terminals_and_a_shrinking_entry` runs from 40x12 down to 1x1 and an out-of-range anchor. It only checks that nothing panics.
- **D-08, Rust:** `live_view::tests::a_silent_provider_is_reported_with_its_silence_and_the_way_out_and_clears_when_data_arrives` covers 100 and 40 columns, tiny sizes, and clearing on a delta, a resume event and the end of the turn.
- **D-08, TS:** `cli/test/stream_stall.test.ts` has 4 cases on an injectable clock, with no sleeps.
- **D-08, TS:** `cli/test/sidecar_projection.test.ts` now classifies the new `stream.stall` event.

The pre-fix failure output for the TS cases is in `docs/evidence/v1.1.4/tui/D-08/unit-red-before.txt`. The D-02 and Rust D-08 red failures were seen in the run and are described in the READMEs, not saved as files.

## Evidence

Before and after screen captures, plus a README on how each was produced, are in `docs/evidence/v1.1.4/tui/D-02/` and `docs/evidence/v1.1.4/tui/D-08/`. The tmux and fake-server harness ran on this host. "Before" is `origin/v1/v114-int-3` built separately.

## Review

I ran the three Entire prompts verbatim as a read-only reviewer on `85fda7fd`.
- **Code review:** 3 findings, all fixed in `737a5e62`.
  - The stall notice stayed on screen when a stalled request ended through a retry, failover or idle timeout. The watchdog now clears it.
  - Non-streaming requests on the same client could raise false stall notices. The notice is now armed only for streaming requests.
  - A very large `LOCAL_LLM_STALL_NOTICE_MS` made the timer fire at once. It is now clamped.
- **Security review:** 0 findings.
- **Slop:** rated Low. The reviewer noted the D-02 test only compares the first `row 0…` line, so a drift inside a wrapped line would REDACTED.

That was the only review round; I did not re-run the reviewer on the final head.

## Residual risks and limits

- **D-02 resize:** if a resize rewraps the anchored line itself, the window stays on that line but may show a different row of it. Only width changes that wrap short lines identically are tested.
- **D-02, untested:** an anchored entry replaced by compaction or `/clear` while its index stays valid, and markdown that reparses earlier lines mid-stream (setext heading, table).
- **D-02, performance:** the anchored entry is now laid out twice per frame, a small extra cost.
- **D-08, keep-alives:** a provider that sends only keep-alive comments or reasoning chunks counts as sending data, so it never shows the notice.
- **D-08, REPL:** only the TUI shows the notice; the REPL does not.
- **D-08, handler:** `onStreamStall` is set on the active client each step and never unset. This is harmless now that only streaming requests use it.
- **Host setup:** I copied built binaries into the worktree's ignored `core/target/debug/` for the PTY run. Nothing there is committed.</result>

<result>**REQUEST_CHANGES** at head `737a5e6267f48a9a88dc3654ef40b2d012df8ec4`.

I confirmed `origin/v1/v114-tui-scroll-stall` is exactly this SHA. This was read-only: nothing was built or run, and the diff base was `origin/v1/v114-int-3`. There are no high or medium code defects. The REQUEST_CHANGES is for two small truthfulness fixes in the docs and test comment, plus one performance suggestion, under the owner's zero-open-findings rule. Everything below is low severity.

## Code Review

I found no defects the Change introduces in either fix.

**D-02, scroll anchor (`live_view.rs`, `live_state.rs`)**
- **Anchor arithmetic:** the new anchor `(entry, line, row_in_line)` is derived correctly. `to_bottom - reach` is the row offset from the line's start, and `from_line - row_in_line + below - height` is the matching inverse. `to_bottom &gt;= reach` is guaranteed by `find`, so there is no underflow.
- **Tiny terminals and clamping:** every subtraction is saturating or `.min()`-clamped. A stale `line_index` falls back to the last line, and slicing `lines[line_index..]` on an empty entry is safe.
- **Streaming growth:** growth below the anchor, and a partial last line that wraps further, do not move the window.
- **Approvals untouched:** nothing in `seen_through`, `approval_drawn` or the approval scroll code changed. `note_layout` only had its tuple type widened.
- **Follow mode and badge:** unchanged. Anchoring is still skipped while follow has a target, and the unseen flag still resets at `scroll_back == 0`.
- **Not covered (admitted in the status row):**
  - an entry replaced at the same index (compaction, `/clear` keeps the index valid);
  - a markdown reparse that shifts earlier lines mid-stream.
- **Resize and tests:** the resize limitation is stated accurately. The test would fail on the old end-relative anchor: top `row 0013` drifts as 60+ rows stream in.

**D-08, stream watchdog (`client.ts` and the event path)**
- **Timer lifecycle:** every exit of the streaming and native paths calls `done()`. The 401 retry path calls it before recursing. `done()` clears the timer and sends `stalled:false` if it was stalled.
- **Process lifetime:** the real timer is `unref`'d, so no leaked timer keeps the process alive.
- **No false stall:** `chatCompletion` (non-streaming) never arms it, and the native path REDACTEDes `stream`. Test 4 asserts this.
- **Injected clock:** used consistently for set, clear and now.
- **Env var parsing:** `LOCAL_LLM_STALL_NOTICE_MS` reuses `parseNum`, so NaN, negative or empty values fall back to the 30 s default. `0` turns it off. Huge values are clamped by `Math.min(stallMs, MAX_TIMER_MS)`, so they cannot hit the Node "fires at once" case.
- **Compatibility:**
  - The Rust `Event` enum has `#[serde(other)] Unknown`, so an old TUI ignores the new event.
  - An old sidecar sending none leaves `stall` as `None`.
  - `sidecar_projection` classifies the new event.
- **Secrets:** the notice carries only a flag and a number of seconds, so no URL or credential can leak.
- **REPL unchanged:** the REPL observer lacks the hook.
- **TUI clearing:** the stall clears on delta, tool start, activity, idle `session.state` and an explicit resume. `silent_secs + elapsed` uses saturating arithmetic.

## Security Review
No vulnerabilities. The only data on the new path is a bool and a `u64`. Rust parses it with defaults, and `silent_secs` is added with `saturating_add`.

## Slop: Low
The two strongest reasons are the status row and test comment overstating what is covered (below), and the doc comment on `render_transcript` going stale. Otherwise the change is minimal, it reuses the existing watchdog and event plumbing, and the tests are real.

## Findings
1. **Low, docs.** `docs/v1.1.4/TUI-AUDIT-STATUS.md` (D-08 row, commit column). It lists only `f5ca1480`, but `737a5e62` is a code commit that changed `client.ts` and the test. The row's "3 cases" is also wrong: `cli/test/stream_stall.test.ts` has 4 tests (the non-streaming guard was added in `737a5e62`). Fix: change the commit column to `f5ca1480`, `737a5e62` and the count to 4. `D-08/README.md` says "2 of 3 fail" for the red run, which is correct for the original 3 tests but worth a note that the fourth is a guard.
2. **Low, test comment and evidence claim.** `live_view.rs:2050`. The comment says "Narrow terminals still say it, in fewer words", but the text is not shortened. At 40 columns the line is clipped by width and the "esc cancels" hint (the way out) is cut off. The assertion only checks for "no data". There is no 40-column capture in the evidence. Fix: either drop the "fewer words" claim and state that the cancel hint can clip at narrow widths, or shorten the notice when the width is small (for example `no data 44 s`) and assert that `esc` stays visible at 40 columns.
3. **Low, performance.** `live_view.rs:1331-1343`. While anchored, the anchored entry is now laid out in full each frame, once for the anchor and again in the walk loop. `wrapped_rows` also runs over every line from `line_index` onward. The old code never wrapped the anchored entry's lines here, so a very long streaming answer (thousands of lines) costs O(entry) wrapping per frame. The doc comment at line 1296, "the cost follows the window, not the transcript", is now untrue for this entry. Fix: reuse the `lines_of(entry)` result in the walk loop, or cache per-entry row counts keyed by width and length. At least correct the comment.

## Good
- **Tests and evidence:**
  - The D-02 test exercises wrapped lines, partial pieces, three resizes and an out-of-range anchor, and is red on the old anchor.
  - The D-08 tests use an injectable clock, a real HTTP stream and a non-streaming guard.
  - Evidence files match the claims: D-02 row 2 reads `Line 15` in all four after-captures against `58, 113, 148, 148` before, and D-08 shows `no data from the provider for 44 s` at 45 s.
- **Design and style:**
  - The scheduler is injectable and the real timer is `unref`'d.
  - The event is structured-only and backward compatible.
  - The change is minimal and fits the existing patterns.

<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT at `24c22d133b01c05462d5ef3a2d85c675458159d9`.** There are no blockers and no new findings. I read the diff and code only, with nothing built or run, so the code claims below come from reading it; two are limits on what I could confirm, noted at (3) and (4).

1. **Docs.**
   - The D-08 row lists `f5ca1480` and `737a5e62` and says 4 cases. The test file has 4 `it`/`test` entries, which matches.
   - The D-08 README calls the 4th test a guard added in `737a5e62` and not part of the red run.
   - The D-02 row lists `737a5e62` as "test comment only". True: that commit changed only a comment in the Rust test, and its other three files are `cli/src/client.ts`, `cli/test/stream_stall.test.ts` and `docs/v1.1.4/TUI-AUDIT-STATUS.md` (the stall work for D-08).

2. **Narrow stall wording.**
   - `fit_activity_line` tries the full line, then ` no data N s · esc cancels `, then ` no data N s `.
   - It measures with `chars().count()`, the same measure as the existing hint code. `·` and the spinner glyphs are 1 column each, so it is correct here.
   - The arithmetic saturates (`saturating_sub(15)` for the `· enter steers ` suffix, which is exactly 15 characters).
   - At 40 columns, `max_hint` is 36 and the compact text is 28 characters. With a draft typed, 21 columns remain, so it falls to ` no data 42 s ` plus the 15-character steer suffix, 29 in total. Both fit, with no ellipsis.
   - At tiny widths the last-resort text can exceed `max`. The caller at lines 181–190 still caps it: it truncates when `max_hint &gt; 3` and otherwise the block clips it. The (1,1), (10,2) and (40,1) draws are retained in the test.

3. **Anchored layout done once.**
   - The anchored entry's `lines` are stored in `anchor_lines` and moved into the walk loop when `index` matches. Otherwise the stored value is put back and `lines_of(index)` runs.
   - `lines_of` is the same call with the same input, so the result is identical to before. `back` and `anchored` are computed before the move, and the D-02 tests assert the top row.
   - The `take()` and put-back leaves no borrow or clone problem that I can see, but I could not compile it to confirm.
   - The cost comment and the status row now fit. The anchored entry is laid out once, entries below it are laid out twice, and the walk stops after filling the window.
   - The 500 ms per-frame bound is generous against a roughly 5000-row layout. The status row says it guards against runaway cost, not the single layout, which is honest. Any flakiness would only show on a very starved runner.

4. **Test fixes.**
   - `top_row` now matches `row `, and the test asserts `top.contains("row 4")`. An empty top row would fail, so it is not vacuous.
   - The narrow test's `!contains('…')` can fail if the text is cut.
   - The small risk is that `row ` could match unrelated screen text in other `top_row` users. I could not run them to check.

5. **`esc cancels` during an approval.**
   - Not reachable. In `composer_hint` the approval arms (`Overlay::Approval`, `CheckpointChoice`, `approval_waiting()`) come before the busy arms that call `fit_activity_line`. While an approval is open or waiting, the hint is the approval text and the stall wording is never shown.
   - The stall is also cleared by any `Activity` event, including the approval phase, by `AssistantDelta`, `ToolStart` and the end of the turn.
   - A stale `esc cancels` next to an approval would need a stall arriving while `pending_approvals` is non-empty, and the hint ordering hides it even then. Severity: none.

6. **Production behaviour.** Only the compact stall wording and the single layout of the anchored entry changed. The `stall_secs` extraction is behaviour-neutral. The rest is tests and docs.

**D-09**
- `shift+enter` is advertised, in the footer and in help, only when the terminal answered the kitty keyboard query at startup.

**D-10**
- Mouse capture is on by default and asks the terminal only for button presses and the wheel (modes 1000 and 1006). `NEXUS_TUI_MOUSE=off` (also `0`, `false`, `no`, `none`) turns it off.
- The wheel scrolls the transcript three rows per notch, or the open panel. A click selects a tool call or edit card, and a second click opens it.
- No mouse event can answer an approval. During an approval the wheel scrolls the review only, and mouse events do not count as typing, so the #469 guards are untouched. A test compares a run with and without mouse events.
- Input modes are switched off on normal quit, on SIGTERM, on an error return, in a panic hook (main thread only), and in the stop watchdog before it force-exits. They are switched off before the slow sidecar shutdown, and stray reports are drained.

**Entire review**
- It was run on `67211d1b` as a read-only subagent. It found no way for a key or mouse event to decide an approval, and no escape-sequence injection.
- It found 9 minor or nit items. I fixed these in `d5f2fbce`:

**Residual risks**
- The xterm `modifyOtherKeys` encoding (`ESC[27;2;13~`, tmux `extended-keys`) is not enabled or parsed, because crossterm 0.29 does not read it. Those terminals count as unsupported and are told to use `alt+enter` or `ctrl+j`.
- A terminal that can't report Shift+Enter sends a plain Enter for it, which sends the draft. That can't be detected, only not advertised.
- Not tested on real kitty, iTerm2, VS Code, tmux or screen; the pty harness stands in for them. tmux needs `mouse on` to REDACTED events and wasn't run.
- No suspend handling exists. Ctrl+Z is a plain key in raw mode, and an external SIGTSTP leaves the modes on while stopped.
- The panic path is not exercised in the pty, and the card highlight is a colour that the text dumps don't show.
- `LiveLayout` lost `Copy`, so any other branch that copies it will need a `.clone()`. That is a small merge-conflict risk, including with other agents editing `approval_sweep.rs`.</result>

## Changes

<result>**REQUEST_CHANGES** at head `92a68252600a4c746672346eec6e2cbd45703bfa`, which I confirmed matches `origin/v1/v114-tui-provider-firstrun`. I read the diff against `origin/v1/v114-int-3` and the code it touches. Nothing was built or run.

## Code Review

**1. HIGH: a 401/403 on the `/models` probe now hard-blocks every prompt.**
- Location: `cli/src/core/provider_recovery.ts:313`, gate at `cli/src/sidecar.ts:469-483`.
- Trigger: `probeAvailability` returns `available:true, httpStatus:401/403` for any status below 500. `startupEndpointProblem` turns that into `kind:"rejected"`, so `providerProblem` is set.
- The probe path never calls `prepareCredential` or `withOAuthRetry` (`client.ts:932-975`), so it never refreshes an expired or not-yet-loaded OAuth token. The chat path does refresh on 401.
- Restricted keys can also 403 on `/models` while chat works.
- Impact: for an expired OAuth token (chatgpt, copilot) or a chat-only key, every prompt is refused with `NO_PROVIDER`. The re-probe repeats the same 401 and never clears the gate. Before this change the 401 was only a warning, and the turn then refreshed the token. Confidence is about 0.7, because I did not confirm whether `credentialFor` pre-loads the OAuth token.
- Fix: gate only on `kind:"unreachable"`. Keep "rejected" as a notice, or let the turn run once and let the real chat path decide. Add a test for a 401 on `/models` followed by a chat request that succeeds.

**2. MEDIUM: a message typed while the gate is running starts an ungated turn.**
- Location: `cli/src/sidecar.ts:338-368`, `:466-483`.
- The gate holds the job busy for up to about 6 s (up to 3 s waiting for the start-up probe, then up to 3 s for the re-probe), and the TUI sends a second message as `steer` while busy.
- During that window `agent.activeTurn` is unset, so `queueSteering` returns `no_active_turn`. The message goes into `followUps` and is acked as "deferred" ("The turn is finishing…").
- When the refused job ends, `runJob.finally` calls `startFollowUp`, which calls `this.turn` directly. That is the retry loop the gate was meant to prevent.
- The author admitted steering and follow-ups are not gated, but this is reachable in ordinary use, not only after a refusal. The line at 338, where steer starts a turn when nothing is running, is also ungated.
- Fix: put the gate in one helper, for example `gatedTurn(id, text, signal)`, and use it in `submit`, `steer` (338) and `startFollowUp` (368). Alternatively, refuse a steer with `NO_PROVIDER` while `providerProblem` is set.

**3. LOW: the history is polluted and the ack is wrong for refused prompts.**
- `submit` pushes to `this.history` (`sidecar.ts:468`) before the gate. A refused prompt therefore lands in prompt history even though the draft is restored. Push it after the gate REDACTEDes.

**4. LOW: draft restore re-inserts the expanded paste.**
- `live_state.rs:1074`: `sent_prompt` holds the expanded text from `composer.take()`. A restored draft that held paste buffers becomes a raw wall of text instead of a paste label. This is acceptable but should be documented.

**Checked and fine:**
- A cancelled re-probe is handled correctly. The abort makes `probeAvailability` throw, the agent's `catch` returns `undefined`, and `if (signal.aborted) return` keeps the gate.
- The 3 s bound holds.
- The startup probe's `.catch` means it never rejects.
- The draft is restored only when the composer is empty and the prompt id matches.
- `put_status` stops at `User` and `Banner`, so a new turn gets its own row. It mutates in place, so entry indices do not shift.
- The old TUI ignores unknown fields. The new TUI defaults `key` and `detail`. The parse test covers both.
- The "gives up in" text uses `record.policy.deadlineMs`, which is the effective deadline: 20 minutes, or the minimum with 2 minutes before first contact (`provider_recovery.ts:339`, `:467`). That is honest. `Retry N` counts failed submissions, so "Retry 1" is the first failure's wait. That is slightly loose but acceptable.

## Security Review

No credential reaches the screen, the saved session or the protocol on the paths I traced.
- Every `notice` goes through `emit` → `formatSafeSidecarEvent` (`sidecar.ts:1227`). That applies `redactForPersistence`, then `redactSecretPatterns`, then `visibleControls`, then the field caps.
- `detail` is also redacted at construction (`protocol.ts:386`, `:434`).
- `detail` is added to the `FIELD_LIMITS` and `sidecar_projection` coverage lists.
- The endpoint shown to the operator comes from `persistableEndpoint`, which strips userinfo, query and fragment (`provider_recovery.ts:312`). The retry-status endpoint (`record.endpoint`) is also the stripped form.
- The TUI keeps `provider_cause` in memory only, and the `/details` output renders as `Output`.

Residual, **LOW** (confidence about 0.4, unverified):
- The `user:REDACTED@host` probe error. A configured endpoint with userinfo stays in `client.endpoints` (`client.ts:614-639`), and Node's fetch rejects such a URL with a message that contains it. `detail` is `err.message` plus the cause chain (`client.ts:1522`).
- The `DATABASE_URL_PASSWORD` pattern (`egress.ts:969`) redacts the REDACTEDword in `REDACTED`, so that case is covered. The username remains.
- A token-only userinfo like `https://TOKEN@host` has no colon, so that pattern does not match. It depends on the entropy classifier.
- The tests only cover `sk-…` in provider messages.
- Fix: add a test with `REDACTED` and `https://TOKENVALUE@host/v1?key=…` that asserts no secret in `detail` or `text`. If the username or token leaks, run `detail` through `persistableEndpoint`-style URL stripping.

## Slop: **Medium**

The two strongest reasons:
1. The central assumption in finding 1, that a 401 on `/models` means unusable, is wrong for real providers. The gate was also applied to only one of the three turn-start paths.
2. The tests do not cover the new risk areas. There is no test for a `/models` 401 followed by a chat request that works, none for a steer or follow-up during the gate, none for a cancelled re-probe, and none for a URL-with-credentials probe error.

The rest is clean and tight, with little comment noise.

**Tests (hunt item 6):** the tests would fail without the fix, because the wording, `key`, `detail` and `NO_PROVIDER` are all new. The rewritten assertions still protect the behaviour. `provider_wait_resume.test.ts` still asserts a wait notice appears before the request, and `provider_recovery_agent.test.ts` still asserts the seconds, the preserved work and the discarded partial output.

**LOW: flake risk in the dead-endpoint test** (`provider_status_plain.test.ts`, the `for (i&lt;200) setImmediate` loop).
- Yielding 200 times is not a clock, so a loaded runner may run out of iterations.
- The next `await send(...)` already awaits `startupProbe` through the gate, so the loop only guards the early assertion on the notice.
- Fix: replace it with a deadline-based poll, for example `const t=Date.now(); while(!found &amp;&amp; Date.now()-t&lt;5000) await new Promise(r=&gt;setTimeout(r,10))`. Alternatively `await (server as any).startupProbe`.

**Docs:**
- The D-06 and D-07 rows and evidence READMEs match the claims.
- The limitations are honest. They name the ungated steering, and the retry bound as a time limit rather than an attempt count.
- LOW: the rows cite commit `e4d9b1fd`, but the head is `92a68252`, which includes the later "gated before the start-up probe ends" follow-up.
- The D-07 row says "4 cases", but there are 6 tests across the "D-07" and "D-07 review follow-ups" describes. The follow-up cases are not counted.
- Update the rows. Also state that a 401 on `/models` blocks prompts, if finding 1 is not fixed.

**Good:**
- A single redaction chokepoint.
- Fully optional protocol fields, with parse tests on the Rust side.
- The `signal.aborted` guard on the re-probe.
- Honest deadline text taken from the effective policy.
- Width tests at 40, 60 and 100 columns.
- Raw causes kept out of the visible text.

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>## Review of `v1/v114-tui-input` at head 10116153f60cf913a0b1d8b2988c04ab7f9bc46b

I confirmed `origin/v1/v114-tui-input` resolves to the full SHA above. I read the diff against `origin/v1/v114-int-3` (86687e2a), the new tests, the evidence files and the docs. I ran no build or tests. Verdict: **REQUEST_CHANGES**. Nothing here weakens approval safety; the one required change is a terminal-cleanliness fix (Finding 1).

### Blockers
None for approval safety. Item 1 under "Safety checks" has the details.

### Findings

**1. Medium (confidence about 0.75) — `core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live.rs:434`: stray mouse reports can still reach the shell**
- Trigger: the user quits while a wheel or trackpad flood is in progress, most likely over SSH. The sequence is:
  - `restore_input_modes()` writes the mouse-off sequences.
  - The single `poll(ZERO)` drain runs immediately.
  - Reports already in flight, one round trip behind, arrive after the drain.
  - `app.handle(Action::Quit)` at line 435 then runs the slow sidecar shutdown with raw mode still on, so those bytes sit unread in the tty input buffer.
  - `drop(guard)` restores cooked mode, and the shell reads `[&lt;64;20;10M…` as typed text.
- The change's own comment names this exact failure ("reports arriving meanwhile would reach the shell as text"), and the docs and status row say "stray reports drained". The code only drains what had already arrived at that instant.
- Fix: drain again after `app.handle(Action::Quit)` and just before `drop(terminal)`. Make the drain a short loop, for example `poll(Duration::from_millis(30))` until it returns false. On unix, `libc::tcflush(STDIN_FILENO, TCIFLUSH)` just before `ratatui::restore()` is the simplest guarantee.
- The D-10 evidence does not exercise this.

**2. Low (confidence about 0.6) — a split mouse report becomes a lone Esc key**
- Where: `live.rs:403-440` (the new continuous stream of `ESC[&lt;…M` reports).
- Trigger: a read boundary falls right after the `ESC` byte of a report, for example from SSH packet splitting during a trackpad flood.
  - crossterm 0.29 treats a lone `ESC` at the end of a read as the Esc key.
  - The rest (`[&lt;64;…M`) then arrives as `Char` keys.
  - Esc at an approval denies by design. Esc during a busy turn sets `cancel_requested` (the `KeyCode::Esc if self.session.busy` arm in `live_state.rs`).
  - The leftover characters land in the draft.
- It cannot approve anything: the leftover bytes are `[`, `&lt;`, digits, `;` and `M`/`m`, never `y`, `n` or `a`.
- Mouse capture makes this much more reachable than before, since arrow keys were the only ESC-led input.
- Fix: no clean fix inside crossterm. Say in `docs/USER_MANUAL.md` and in the help row that `NEXUS_TUI_MOUSE=off` is the remedy on high-latency links. Optionally coalesce queued wheel events in the loop to shrink the exposure.

**3. Low — `core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live.rs:~640` (wheel redraws)**
- Every wheel notch triggers a full `draw`. Consecutive wheel events in the queue could be summed into one redraw.

**4. Low — `docs/evidence/v1.1.4/tui/D-09/red.txt`: stale**
- It lists `a_stale_layout_never_panics_or_selects_the_wrong_entry`, but the final test is `a_stale_layout_never_panics_or_selects_a_row_that_is_no_longer_a_tool_card` (and the status row cites the latter).
- It lists none of the `live::tests` mouse-event test (`a_mouse_event_is_not_typing_and_does_not_split_a_burst`).
- Re-capture the red run from the final tests.
- Several "red" entries REDACTED at red, which is expected for safety invariants. The ten that fail do prove the new behaviour.

**5. Low — docs/status overclaim**
- `docs/USER_MANUAL.md` asserts "kitty, WezTerm, foot, Ghostty" answer the query, but no real terminal was run (the evidence says so).
- The manual says nothing about tmux or screen. The status row mentions `set -g mouse on`, but only the status row does.
- On Windows, `EnableMouseCapture` reports motion and drag, so "asks only for presses and wheel" is true only on unix.
- Soften the claim, add the tmux/screen line to the manual, and say the restriction is unix-only.

### Safety checks (traced, no defect found)

1. **Approval safety**
   - No mouse event can decide an approval.
   - `on_mouse` → `handle_mouse` returns `None` and only scrolls `Approval.scroll`.
   - The event loop forwards only the wheel and left-button-down.
   - A mouse event never touches `last_key`, `last_text`, `guard_until`, `seen_through` or `approval_drawn`.
   - `on_key_at` sets `last_key` only for key codes, and `on_paste_at` is unchanged.
   - The wheel redraw does not change the opening guard, because `note_approval_shown` only fires on the first draw.
   - A mouse event queued as an approval opens is discarded and is not "typing". Inside bursts it is dropped, so it neither splits a burst nor counts toward one (`drain_key_presses`, the paste-burst arm).
   - Ctrl+J in raw mode arrives as Char('j') with CONTROL, so it is neither `bare` nor `letter` and does nothing at an approval.
   - Alt+Enter is not bare, so it does nothing. A Shift+Enter that arrives as plain Enter behaves as before.
   - With only DISAMBIGUATE_ESCAPE_CODES pushed, there are no release or repeat events.
   - Click-opening a detail panel is the same as Enter, and clicks are ignored while any overlay is open.
   - Byte-echoed `ESC[&lt;…M` characters are never y, n or a. The Esc caveat is Finding 2.

2. **Terminal restore**
   - The enable and disable sequences match (1000+1006 on; 1006, 1015, 1003, 1002 and 1000 off).
   - The keyboard-protocol push and pop both happen inside the alternate screen, so they are symmetric.
   - The `AtomicBool` swaps make the restore idempotent.
   - Normal return, error return (`?` after the flags are set), unwinding and the main-thread panic hook all restore.
   - SIGTERM and SIGHUP restore through the loop; the watchdog restores with raw `write(2)` calls.
   - The crash screen keeps the modes on harmlessly and ignores the mouse.
   - Ctrl+Z is a plain key in raw mode. An external SIGTSTP leaves the modes on while stopped, and the status row says so. That is acceptable.

3. **Kitty query**
   - The call is unchanged. crossterm waits up to 2 s, and a DA1 reply returns false immediately.
   - The query runs before mouse capture is enabled, so mouse reports cannot interleave with it.

4. **`NEXUS_TUI_MOUSE`**
   - `mouse_enabled` parsing is correct and trim- and case-insensitive.
   - Its test covers it.

5. **Escape-sequence injection**
   - The new help and hint text is static, and the click opens the already-sanitised detail panel.

6. **cfg split**
   - `WheelAndClick` and the unlocked restore are unix-only, with Windows fallbacks.
   - `MouseButton` and `MouseEventKind` are imported on both.
   - `libc` is used only on unix.
   - Nothing fails to compile.

7. **`LiveLayout` losing `Copy`**
   - The only copying sites were `approval_sweep.rs:138` and `:192`, both now `.clone()`.
   - `approval_sweep.rs:317` (`let layout = frame.layout;`) is a legal partial move of an owned `Frame`.
   - `input_sweep.rs:52` and `live.rs:460` move it once.
   - Merging with `origin/v1/v114-tui-scroll-stall`: `git merge-tree` shows conflicts in `live_view.rs` and `docs/v1.1.4/TUI-AUDIT-STATUS.md`. The overlapping `live_view.rs` hunks are:
     - `render_transcript`: the `tail` tuple widening, `top` becoming a triple, and the `top` computation next to the new `row_entries` loop after `tail.reverse()`.
     - The `composer_hint` area.
     - The `tests` module tail.
   - `live_state.rs` auto-merges: `top` and `anchor` become triples, and `note_layout` takes `layout.top` unchanged.
   - My `row_entries` loop destructures `(_, rows, index, ..)`, so it survives the tuple change semantically.
   - Only the `row_entries` placement needs a manual merge.

### Slop: Low
- The strongest reasons are that the implementation is proportionate and the tests assert real invariants. The pty evidence is honest, and the harness uses the existing driver.
- The small debris is the stale `red.txt`, near-duplicate `@newline` help arms, and the unverified terminal list in the manual.

### Good
- Mouse events are never typing, and a test compares the guard behaviour with and without mouse events.
- `no_mouse_event_anywhere_answers_an_approval` sweeps every mouse kind, modifier and cell.
- Each mode flag is set before its sequence is written, so a half-failed write is still undone.
- The capture is restricted to 1000+1006, which keeps Shift-drag selection working and avoids motion floods.
- The pty evidence records the exact mode bytes at start, quit, SIGTERM and with the flag off.</result>

<status>completed</status>

<summary>Agent "Delta review of TUI D-09/D-10 at b85f3175" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**ACCEPT at head `b85f3175f1f40690812d63491af84ecec787fcdf`** (`origin/v1/v114-tui-input`). This is read-only: I ran no builds or tests and judged from the code and committed evidence. The Medium from `10116153` is resolved, and I found no blockers and no approval-safety regression.

**(1) Exit sequence**
- `run_live` stores the `event_loop` result first, so normal quit and error return both go through `end_session`. The order is modes off, drain, sidecar shutdown, drain.
- Each drain is bounded by count (4096), by a 300 ms deadline, and by a 30 ms quiet poll that ends it early. A flood cannot spin it. Worst case on quit is about 0.3 s + 3 s (`SHUTDOWN_GRACE`) + 0.3 s, well inside the 7 s watchdog.
- `restore_input_modes` swaps `MOUSE_ON`, `KEYBOARD_ON` and `PASTE_ON` with `AtomicBool`, so the mouse-off, keyboard-pop and paste-off sequences are written only once. The guard drop, panic hook, watchdog and `end_session` can all call it without printing garbage.
- Panic: the hook restores modes on the main thread, then unwinding runs the guard drop, which flushes stdin and calls `ratatui::restore()`.
- SIGTERM/SIGHUP: the loop sees the stop and takes the normal path. If it is stuck, the watchdog writes the raw restore, calls `tcflush`, and force-exits.
- Crash screen: it is only a link state, so quit follows the same path.
- Windows: `flush_stdin` and the `tcflush` in `restore_input_modes_unlocked` are `cfg(unix)`. `libc` is a `target.'cfg(unix)'` dependency, and the non-unix `restore_input_modes_unlocked` is empty.
- The drop-in-guard flush runs before `ratatui::restore()`. Lost type-ahead is documented in the manual.

**(2) `apply_wheel_run`**
- Each wheel notch scrolls, the screen is drawn once, and the first non-wheel event goes into `pending` and is never dropped or reordered.
- The run is capped at 512 events. The leftovers are handled next iteration after a redraw, so neither the redraw nor the loop can be starved.
- A kept event takes the existing `pending` route, which skips the poll and `read`.
- No sidecar `pump` runs inside the wheel run, so an approval cannot open mid-run. At the next loop top, `pump_guarding_approvals` still sees `pending` and discards it as typed-before-the-panel if an approval opened. A key behind a wheel run therefore reaches the approval logic as before, via `drain_key_presses`.
- `on_mouse` still never decides an approval, and a wheel run is not counted as typing.

**(3) pty evidence**
- The control for `10116153` shows the shell reading the report text. `after.txt` shows an empty read. The reader demonstrably works, so the empty result is a real negative.
- The `read -n 300` cap truncates the control, which does not matter.
- The README states the residual honestly: reports sent after exit, the pty continuing to send after modes are off, and no latency test. Three runs are claimed but only one is saved.

**(4) `red.txt`**
- It lists 17 FAILED lines, matching "317 REDACTEDed; 17 failed". The header explains that the approval-guard tests REDACTED on the stubs by construction.

**(5) Docs, status row and help text**
- The terminal claims are limited to "from the code and the protocols, not from runs". The tmux/screen line, the unix-only "presses and wheel" wording and the `NEXUS_TUI_MOUSE=off` remedy are all present.
- The manual's "never approves anything" wording is carefully qualified. A split report leaves Esc plus `[ &lt; digits ; M/m`, none of which is `y`, `n` or `a`.

**(6) Approval safety**
- The opening guard, repeat window and `seen_through` are untouched, and no mouse or key path can decide an approval.

**Nits (not blocking)**
- `docs/v1.1.4/TUI-AUDIT-STATUS.md` D-10 row says "so reports do not reach the shell" without the in-flight-after-drains residual that the manual and README state. Add "(reports still in flight after the second drain can)" to the row.
- `red.txt` says "round 2 head" rather than a SHA.
- Only one of the three clean runs is saved.
- `apply_wheel_run`'s unused `Option&lt;Action&gt;` return and the unnamed constants were already accepted.

**Verdict: ACCEPT** at 4754812dd5006906bfda500e154ef3a460815ed1. No blockers, and all four findings are fixed in the code.

### Blockers
No blockers found.

### Verification
- **(a) Isolation fact.**
  - `scheduler.ts:99` uses `Boolean(this.state.git?.isolate)`, and `operation_runner.ts:121` builds `OperationGit` only on `state.git?.isolate`. These are the same predicate.
  - `state.git` is set at a single place, `operation_cli.ts:267-272`, and only when `spec.git.isolate` is set. The runner constructs `OperationGit` from persisted state on resume, so the two cannot diverge.
  - Hand-edited or older state without `git` is treated as non-isolated: closed by default, and correct.
  - Only `failure_debugger` can be admitted. `dependencySatisfied` has `u.role === "failure_debugger"` as a hard conjunct. The new test sweeps six roles in both modes.
  - A debugger cannot run in an isolated operation without its snapshot. `prepareUnit` runs before the executor. If it throws, the unit returns `failed` with a retry option and never runs. `ctx.workspace` is only set after `prepareUnit` succeeds.
  - A debugger with no BLOCKED dependency in an isolated operation gets an ordinary unit worktree, never the shared workspace.
  - The `recoverMerged` path returns `failed` before the executor is called.
- **(b) Dropped field.**
  - `scopeChange` was introduced by `5855c3c8` on this branch and is in no release tag, so no v1.1.3 state can contain it.
  - Nothing else reads it; `rg` finds no other references in `src`, `test` or `docs`.
  - Persisted operation state is validated structurally, so an unknown field on a decision would not be rejected anyway.
  - The A4 row edit is truthful. The `DECISION_APPLIED` message includes the note, and the rewritten test asserts it with a regex on the message.
- **(c) Retry removal.**
  - The only other `retry` offers are in different branches: the prepare-failure path (a transient failure, where retry is legitimate) and the executor-error path.
  - With `options: []`, `block()` appends exactly one terminal option. A required unit gets `cancel_operation` and an advisory unit gets `drop`.
  - `resolve()` rejects `drop` for a required unit, so the two cases are consistent.
  - Both terminals are tested to end the block: the required case ends in `state.status === "CANCELLED"`, and the advisory case sets `dropped` and clears `blocked`.
- **(d) Tests are meaningful.**
  - The isolated/non-isolated sweep covers six roles, so it would fail if either the isolation conjunct or the role check were removed.
  - The non-isolated runner test asserts the debugger stays `WAITING_ON_DEPENDENCY` and the executor is never called.
  - The listing-count test uses a PATH git shim to count `ls-files --others` calls (exactly one) and also asserts the diff content is unchanged.
  - The `debuggerDeps` deepEqual strengthens the snapshot test by pinning `failed: true`.
  - The two `operation_release_acceptance` failures are pre-existing, as you state; I did not re-run them.
- **(e) Docs and comments.** The A4 row, the `operation.ts` lifecycle comment, `scheduler.ts:40-46` and the `operation_runner.ts` comments all agree with the code.

### Suggestions (none blocks)
- **The author-admitted drop deadlock, non-isolated operation, `scheduler.ts:488-490` and `:102`.**
  - Trigger: an advisory unit fails and is dropped. A drop sets `dropped = true` but leaves the unit `BLOCKED`, which is in `TERMINAL`, so it is never `COMPLETE`.
  - A required unit that depends on it then stays `WAITING_ON_DEPENDENCY`. `decide()` falls through to `{kind:"blocked", decisions:[]}`, so the operation is stuck with nothing to decide.
  - Severity: low to medium, not new to this delta. It is pre-existing for any required unit depending on a dropped unit, and the delta only adds one more way to reach it. A required debugger can only be waiting on a failed unit in a non-isolated operation when the dropped unit is advisory. A required failed dependency cannot be dropped.
  - Not a blocker, but it should not be left unrecorded. Cheapest fix: record it as a ledger limitation now. A drop that leaves a required dependent unsatisfiable is a small follow-up: in `resolve()` next to line 435, throw `DecisionRejected` when `action.kind === "drop"` and any required unit has `u.id` in `dependsOn`. Check this does not break existing tests that drop a unit with a required dependent.
  - Side effect of the same cause, in isolated mode: a dropped advisory failed unit keeps `status BLOCKED` and `failedRun`, so a debugger can still be admitted on it. This is harmless and arguably desired, but should be recorded with the limitation.
- **Product consequence to note in the ledger.** In a non-isolated operation, a `failure_debugger` after a failed unit can never run. A retry just re-fails the dependency, and the only exits are retry or stop. This is the intended closed default and is documented in A4, but the limitation entry should say so plainly.
- **`cli/test/specialist_auto_inputs.test.ts`, the assertion `"scopeChange" in u.decisions![0]`.** It guards a field that exists nowhere else in the code. It is harmless, but it can go on the next touch.

### Entire briefs on the delta
- **Code:** no defects. The `others` listing now runs before the diff listing; it is still a single read and its use is unchanged.
- **Security:** the delta only narrows admission and does not touch input handling. No finding.
- **Slop: Low.** The two strongest points are that the fixes are minimal and fix the root cause by reusing the runner's own isolation predicate, and that each fix has a test that exercises it. The only nits are the redundant `scopeChange` absence assertion and a slightly long comment in `operation_runner.ts:353`.

### Good
- The admission fix reuses the exact predicate the runner uses, instead of adding a second notion of "isolated".
- The role-by-mode sweep test proves "no other role is ever admitted".
- Each fix has a test that exercises it, as you describe, including the shim-based call counter.

<result>## Review of `v1/v114-tui-provider-firstrun` at `854d267dcafcde4f6e4b29381ab790f710ce7b3f`

**Verdict: ACCEPT.** The diff is `92a68252600a4c746672346eec6e2cbd45703bfa..854d267dcafcde4f6e4b29381ab790f710ce7b3f`. The review was read-only; nothing was built or run. The HIGH and MEDIUM findings from `92a68252` are fixed in code.

### Blockers
No blockers found.

### Suggestions

- **LOW. `cli/src/core/provider_recovery.ts:319`: the "is this a timeout" test reads the detail text.** `blocking = httpStatus === undefined &amp;&amp; !/timeout|timed out|aborted/i.test(detail)` is a regex over a string that also carries the host and cause chain.
  - A refused endpoint at a host such as `api.timeout-llm.com` is classed as a timeout and does not gate.
  - A message like `connect ETIMEDOUT` matches nothing, so it gates. The 3 s `AbortSignal.timeout` fires first in the real probe, so this is theoretical.
  - Fix: add `timedOut?: boolean` to `ProbeResult`, set at `cli/src/client.ts:1524` from `err?.name === "TimeoutError"`, and gate on `httpStatus === undefined &amp;&amp; !timedOut`. At minimum, test the regex against the real `fetch` timeout message, not a hand-written string.
  - Related, same line of reasoning: ECONNRESET, ENETUNREACH and TLS-verification failures also gate, and the refusal says "nothing answers". That is acceptable because chat uses the same `fetch` and would fail the same way, but the TLS wording is misleading.

- **LOW. `cli/src/core/endpoint_identity.ts:60`: `scrubUrls` leaves residue in odd forms.** The pattern `[a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*:\/\/[^\s"'&lt;&gt;]+` stops at a quote or space.
  - `http://u:p'w@h/x` leaves `w@h/x`.
  - `http://u:pa ss@h` leaves `ss@h`.
  - Scheme-less `//user:REDACTED@host` and `user:REDACTED@host:5432/db` are not touched at all. `redactSecretPatterns` has no userinfo rule either.
  - Percent-encoded userinfo is fine, because `new URL` drops the whole userinfo.
  - Non-URL text is safe: a path with `@`, an email, `C:\x` and `file:///x` are unchanged.
  - The one cosmetic corruption is that `persistableEndpoint` lowercases the scheme and host and trims a trailing `/` from a URL inside prose.
  - Fix: add an authority rule, for example `/(\/\/)[^\s/@"'&lt;&gt;]*:[^\s/@"'&lt;&gt;]*@/g` → `$1`, plus a userinfo cut that runs to the last `@` before the first `/`. Add tests for `http://u:p'w@h/x`, `//user:REDACTED@host`, `user:REDACTED@db:5432/x` and `mailto:a@b`, plus a `file:///` unchanged case.

- **LOW. `core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live_state.rs:1957` and `:1075`: restoring the whole `Composer` also restores its recall history.** The clone is taken before `take()` pushes to the composer's history. If the user submits another message in the gate window (a steer, after which the composer is empty again), the restore on `NO_PROVIDER` overwrites that history entry.
  - The restore is guarded by `id` match and `composer.is_empty()`, so typed text is never clobbered. It is empty-only, as required.
  - The cost is only a lost Up-arrow recall entry, and a refused steer returns its text via the ack anyway.
  - Fix: restore only `text`, the pastes and `paste_seq` and the cursor, via a `Composer::restore_draft(&amp;Composer)`, and leave `history` alone. Also, the clone copies the whole history `Vec` on every Enter. A draft-only snapshot avoids that.

- **LOW. `cli/test/provider_status_plain.test.ts`: test gaps.**
  - Cancel during the gate has no test (author-admitted); a cancelled re-probe leaving the gate in place has none either.
    - Risk is low. `cancel()` aborts the signal, and `probeAvailability` rethrows on abort. That throw is caught in `probeEndpointProblem` and returns `undefined`, but `gatedTurn` checks `signal.aborted` first and calls `hooks.refused("Cancelled before it was sent.")`. That runs before `providerProblem` is overwritten.
    - A cheap test: send a prompt to `127.0.0.1:1`, call `cancel` immediately, and assert that exactly one of `error` (`NO_PROVIDER`, message "Cancelled") or `turn.result` is emitted and the gate is kept.
    - One imprecision: a cancel while `startupProbe` is still pending, with no known problem, runs the turn with an aborted signal. That is the old behaviour and it ends as INTERRUPTED.
  - The timeout half of the "503 or timeout" test checks only a hand-written detail string, not the real probe message.
  - The URL test has no scheme-less or quote cases.

- **LOW. `docs/v1.1.4/TUI-AUDIT-STATUS.md`: the commit column does not list real hashes.** It says "round-2 commit (this push)", but the two commits are `3539b9f6` and `854d267d`.
  - Replace the placeholder with `3539b9f6`, `854d267d`.
  - The D-06 "8 cases" count includes "recovery keeps the earlier error", which sits in the "D-07 review follow-ups" describe block. It should be cited by test name.

### Verification of your questions

1. **Gate predicate.**
   - Only `probe.available === false` with no `httpStatus` and no timeout wording blocks. 401, 403, 429, 5xx, 408 and timeouts only warn: `providerProblem` is not set and `noProviderNotice` says "Nexus will still try your messages".
   - Re-probing with `problem?.blocking` clears a stale gate.
   - A truly dead endpoint, such as refused, ENOTFOUND or EAI_AGAIN, still gets the "No model is connected / Next: /connect…" notice.
   - A cold local server that is listening but loading returns 503 or times out, so it is a warning only. If the server is not listening yet, the prompt is refused, but the next prompt re-probes.
   - Localhost and IPv6: the probe uses the same `fetch` and `AggregateError` chain as chat, so a working provider is not blocked on a path that chat could not use either.
   - The integration tests drive a real 401/403/503 `/models` against a working chat; they exercise the code and would fail without the fix.

2. **Entry points.** `gatedTurn` is the only caller of `this.turn(` (`sidecar.ts:521`). Its callers are `submit`, `steer` from idle, `startFollowUp`, and the slash `outcome.kind === "turn"` path (`/image`, `/step`, `!N`, `/history run`). `/tdd`, `/team` and `/compact --llm` remain ungated residuals. They are honestly documented and acceptable.
   - A second message in the window goes to the `queueSteering` → `no_active_turn` → deferred path, so nothing is lost. When the gate refuses, `runJob`'s finally runs `startFollowUp`, which gates again (one more 3 s probe at most) and returns `cancelled` with the text. The test asserts `["deferred","cancelled"]`.
   - A cancel in the window returns the message and does not double-send. `cancel()` already acks the deferred follow-ups with their text and clears the list.
   - `/connect` does not clear the gate. Only the next gated turn's re-probe does, and it uses the current `getAgent().client`. A model selection is needed to rebuild it, which the docs state.
   - The `slash.result` announcement for a turn-starting command is now emitted after the gate, so it can lag by up to about 6 s on a dead provider. That is harmless.

3. **URL scrubbing.** See the second suggestion above.

4. **TUI restore.** It is guarded by id match and `composer.is_empty()` (`text.is_empty()`), and a typed-meanwhile test exists. The only effect on the composer beyond its text is the history rollback in the third suggestion. A paste-label test exists and would fail against the old `String` snapshot.

5. **Tests.** The steer, follow-up, `/image`, history and 401/403/503 tests would each fail against the pre-fix code, because the old behaviour was to block, run ungated, or announce the prompt. The `)` URL test REDACTEDes with the regex change. The cancel gap is rated LOW above.

6. **Docs and protocol.** The D-07 count of 13 is correct (4 + 8 + 1). The limitations are honest and match the code: `/tdd`, `/team` and `/compact --llm` ungated, no cancel test, `/connect` needs a model pick, and older TUIs show `NO_PROVIDER: …` without a restore. Protocol compatibility is unchanged: `steer.ack.text` and the `notice` fields are optional, and `NO_PROVIDER` is additive.

### Good
- `gatedTurn` is a single gate with `admitted` and `refused` hooks. History and acks are applied only after admission, which removes the history pollution and the ungated-steer paths together.
- The cancelled-gate case returns the message rather than dropping it, and a cancel with no known problem is left to the turn's own INTERRUPTED path.
- The 401/403 downgrade to a warning is the right call, and the gate now rests on one documented predicate.
- The start-up wait is a 5 s poll (`until`), replacing the 200-iteration `setImmediate` loop.
- `scrubUrls` is applied at both the detail and the status/refusal layers, and the test covers `)`, `ws://`, IPv6 and `?key=#frag`.

**Slop: Low.** The two strongest reasons are the following. The change removes duplicate gate logic into one function and its tests are mostly behavioural (real sidecar, real sockets). The remaining issues are small and isolated: a regex used for classification, the history rollback in the composer restore, and a placeholder commit reference.</result>

<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT** at head `42b6ceca621a9c029e6dd723ae18b148d4bb3f3b`, the delta over `854d267dcafcde4f6e4b29381ab790f710ce7b3f`. I found no Blocker and no Medium. I read the code and ran nothing, so the author's 197/197 TS and 325/325 Rust results are unverified by me. The last commit is docs-only.

## Verification of the five Lows

1. **Timeout flag: fixed.**
   - `cli/src/client.ts:1525` sets `timedOut` only when `err?.name === "TimeoutError"`, the same predicate `classifyTransportFailure` already used.
   - `cli/src/core/provider_recovery.ts:323` gates on `httpStatus === undefined &amp;&amp; !probe.timedOut`, so the text regex is gone.
   - The operator-abort path rethrows before the flag is set, so it cannot be mistaken for a timeout.
   - The real-timeout test uses a `net.createServer` that accepts and never answers, with `timeoutMs` 150 plumbed through `probeEndpointProblem`. It asserts `blocking === false`, so it does exercise the real fetch deadline.
   - The `api.timeout-llm.com` test asserts `blocking === true`.
   - TLS and ECONNRESET now say "could not connect" in both the refusal and the notice. ECONNREFUSED, ENOTFOUND and EAI_AGAIN keep "nothing answers", and the tests cover both.
2. **`scrubUrls`: correct on all listed forms.**
   - I traced each listed case through the three rules, plus the unchanged-text cases and the test fixtures, and the outputs match the test expectations.
   - `http://u:p'w@h/x` becomes `http://h/x`. The greedy `[^\/\n]*` backtracks to the last `@` before a `/`.
   - `http://u:pa ss@h` loses its userinfo, because the class admits a space.
   - `//user:REDACTED@host` and `user:REDACTED@db:5432/x` lose their userinfo.
   - Email, `mailto:a@b`, `/home/a@b/file`, `C:\x`, `file:///x` and `// a comment about a@b` come back unchanged. The third rule's lookahead needs a `/` after the host, and the second rule needs a non-space after `//`.
3. **Draft restore: fixed.** `Draft` holds only text, pastes, `paste_seq` and cursor. `snapshot_draft` per Enter clones those four fields, not the history `Vec`. `restore_draft` leaves history alone and clears `recall` and `stash`. The test proves history made after the send survives.
4. **Cancel-during-gate test: accepted as written.** See Low C.
5. **Docs rows: real commits, honest limits.** `e4d9b1fd`, `92a68252`, `3539b9f6`, `854d267d` and `1145e839` all exist as commits. The D-06 count of 8 matches the file (7 in the D-06 describe plus 1 in follow-ups). The D-07 round-2 count of 12 is consistent with the file. The scrub limits are stated honestly.
6. **No unexplained new production behaviour.** The `timeoutMs` default parameter in `agent.ts` is a test seam that keeps the 3,000 ms default. The `nothingThere` field is optional, and an absent value keeps the old wording.

## Items the author left unfixed

- **(a) A raw `/` in a URL REDACTEDword still leaks.**
  - Trace of `http://u:pa/ss@h/x`: rule 1 cannot cross the `/`. Rule 3 calls `persistableEndpoint`. `new URL` throws on the invalid port `pa`, so `stripUnparseable` takes the authority `u:pa`, which has no `@`. The whole string comes back unchanged, so the full REDACTEDword (`pa` in the authority, `ss` in the path) stays in `detail`.
  - It is not new in this delta. The `854d267d` version of `scrubUrls` (rule 3 only) has the same behaviour. `scrubUrls` does not exist at `origin/v1/v114-int-3`; it arrived with the earlier D-06/D-07 commits on this branch.
  - Base64 REDACTEDwords and API keys really can contain `/`, so a realistic trigger exists. Undici's "Request cannot be constructed from a URL that includes credentials" message embeds the configured URL.
  - Exposure: the user's own configured credential, shown to the same user in a local TUI. It lands in `detail` and `/details`, and possibly in a transcript.
  - `detail` also goes through `redactSecrets` (`cli/src/protocol.ts:387, 436, 443`). That calls `redactForTelemetry` and then `redactSecretPatterns`. I did not read those patterns, so I cannot say whether they cover a bare REDACTEDword in a URL. Treat the leak as unproven to be covered.
  - Severity: Low-to-Medium and documented.
- **(b) Greedy rule 1 over-cuts.** A line like `//host:port ... a@corp` loses the middle. This is diagnostic loss only. Acceptable.
- **(c) Scheme-less `user:REDACTED@host` is cut only when a `/` follows the host.** It leaks only where the original did, and in a bare `user:REDACTED@host` form it is rarely a URL. Acceptable.
- **(d) Quadratic scrub.**
  - Rule 2 starts at any position after a character outside `[\w\/@.:-]`, and its first class admits commas, so `a,a,a,…` rescans the whole run at every comma. The cost is O(n²).
  - Callers:
    - The probe `detail` is `err.message` plus a cause chain capped at 8 entries (`client.ts:496`). An HTTP failure gives only `HTTP &lt;status&gt;`, never the response body, so a hostile body cannot reach it.
    - The recovery path (`protocol.ts:387`) feeds `last.message`. I did not trace whether that message is capped before `scrubUrls`; `boundedProviderText` applies a cap in the client.
  - The practical worst case is a server-controlled TLS certificate name in an error message, a few KB. That means a blocking regex of tens of milliseconds, which is a negligible risk.
- **(e) "bad port" still says "nothing answers".** Cosmetic. In `provider_recovery.ts:324`, `nothingThere` includes `bad port`, so the wording stays "nothing answers" for that case.

## Findings

- **Low A (hardening, optional)** — `cli/src/core/endpoint_identity.ts:25-36`, `stripUnparseable`.
  - Trigger: `https://u:abc/def@host/v1`.
  - Fix: when a `scheme://` token fails to parse and its first segment has a `:` followed by non-digits, cut to the last `@` in the whitespace-delimited token. Add this test case alongside the existing ones.
- **Low B** — `cli/src/core/provider_recovery.ts:324`, `nothingThere` regex over scrubbed `detail`.
  - A hostname containing `econnrefused` or `enotfound` in a TLS error flips the wording to "nothing answers". Wording only. Fix: match on the cause code token with a word-boundary pattern.
- **Low C** — `cli/test/provider_status_plain.test.ts:358-368`, cancel-during-gate test.
  - The probe to `127.0.0.1:1` refuses almost instantly, so the cancel usually arrives after the gate has already refused. The test then REDACTEDes through the `NO_PROVIDER` branch and never reaches the `"Cancelled before it was sent."` path in `sidecar.ts:508`.
  - It still fails on a silently dropped message (zero events fails `refused + results === 1`), on a duplicate report, and on a lost gate. It cannot prove the `signal.aborted` branch. I accept it.
  - Fix, if wanted: probe a hanging local socket so the cancel is guaranteed to land mid-probe, and assert the exact cancelled message.
- **Low D (docs)** — `docs/v1.1.4/TUI-AUDIT-STATUS.md` D-07 row.
  - The prose says the notice reads "nothing answers" for "refused, DNS, TLS, reset", while the same row later says TLS and reset use "could not connect". Fix: state that "nothing answers" applies to refused and DNS only.
- **Note** — Up-arrow recall of a refused prompt: `take()` records the prompt at send time, so it stays in history, and the draft is also restored. I judge this acceptable and mildly helpful, since the operator can re-edit and resend. The only quirk is an adjacent duplicate after resend.
- **Note** — Cursor survival is restored but untested. Low risk, because the text is identical to the snapshot.

## Slop: Low

The two strongest reasons are:
- The change is minimal and targeted. It uses the real error name instead of a text regex, a draft-only snapshot instead of cloning the whole composer, and optional fields for compatibility.
- Tests match their claims, and the docs state their limits.

Residual issues are small: the cancel test cannot force the cancelled path, and one doc sentence is inconsistent (Low D).

**Good**
- Replacing the regex-over-text classification with a structured flag.
- The `scrubUrls` negative test list covers email, mailto, paths, `file:///` and a `//` comment.
- Limits are documented honestly, including the `/` REDACTEDword gap.

<result>**ACCEPT** at head `76c5ae6f72749cc15bcea2701efa4e85b102cb7e`. Blockers: none. This was a read-only review: no build or tests were run, and the worktree HEAD equals the fetched `origin/v1/v114-tui-provider-firstrun`. I read the code against the Code, Security and Slop briefs.

### Blockers
No blockers found.

### Verification
- **(A) Scrub: holds.**
  - The strings `https://u:abc/def@host/v1` and `https://u:abc/def/ghi@host/v1` REDACTED untouched through the first two `scrubUrls` rules. The first rule's `[^\/\n]*@` cannot cross the `/`, and the second's lookbehind blocks `u:abc`.
  - Rule 3 hands the token to `persistableEndpoint`. `new URL` throws on the non-numeric port `abc`, so `stripUnparseable` runs. The `?key=` is cut first. The authority `u:abc` matches `/^[^@:]*:(?!\d+$)/`, and the cut falls at the last `@` in `rest`. The result is `https://host/v1` in all three cases, and the prose case yields the expected string.
  - Look-alikes are unchanged. `https://host/path@x`, `https://host:8080/a@b` and `file:///x` all parse, so they never reach the new branch. The email-in-prose string has no `://`. `https://h:99999/x@y` fails parsing, but `(?!\d+$)` rejects the all-digit "port", so nothing is cut.
  - The test's final loop checks `problem.detail`, `problem.endpoint`, `noProviderNotice(...).detail` (which goes through `redactSecrets` at `protocol.ts:443`), `.text` and `noProviderRefusal`, against `/abc|def/`. That matches the claim.
  - The new regex is anchored and linear, so no catastrophic backtracking comes from it. The O(n²) on long runs is in the existing rule 2/3 patterns, and the D-07 row already says so. `detail` is not length-capped before scrubbing. It is built from Node error messages (the cause chain stops at 8 entries), so I left this as-is.
  - The D-07 limits (all-digit REDACTEDword, `?` or `#` in a REDACTEDword in a directly persisted endpoint, space plus `/`, first-rule over-cut) match the code, so they are honest.
- **(B) Structured code: holds.**
  - `client.ts:1525` carries `codes` from the cause chain (AggregateError members included). `nothingThere` now matches only `ECONNREFUSED`, `ENOTFOUND` or `EAI_AGAIN` in that array.
  - A TLS error with `codes: ["UNABLE_TO_VERIFY_LEAF_SIGNATURE"]` and a host named `econnrefused.example` gives `nothingThere = false` and "could not connect".
  - A probe without `codes` degrades to "could not connect", which is safe.
  - The `bad port` regex is gone. A fetch-banned port now reads "could not connect", and the doc no longer lists it as a limit.
  - The real-refusal test closes a listener, then probes that port. A closed loopback port yields `ECONNREFUSED`, `blocking` is true (no HTTP status and no timeout), and the wording is "nothing answers". The only flake risk is an ephemeral port being reused in the gap, which is negligible.
- **(C) Cancel test: deterministic.**
  - The test awaits `startupProbe` first, which takes 3 s to time out against the silent socket. It sets `providerProblem` itself, then submits. The test waits (a 10 ms poll, not a fixed sleep) until the server has seen a new socket, so the cancel lands while the re-probe is in flight.
  - `runJob` creates the `AbortController` synchronously, so `cancel()` aborts the probe. `probeAvailability` rethrows on abort, `probeEndpointProblem` returns undefined, and `gatedTurn` returns `hooks.refused("Cancelled before it was sent.")`.
  - The test asserts exactly one `NO_PROVIDER` error with that exact message, no `activity` or `turn.result`, and `providerProblem` still the same object. The `finally` destroys the sockets and closes the server.
- **(D) Docs: one count is wrong; the rest is consistent.**
  - Both D-06 and D-07 rows now list `e60421ef`. `76c5ae6f` is test and docs only, so leaving it out is fine.
  - Wording is consistent: "nothing answers" for refused or DNS only, "could not connect" for TLS, reset and anything else.
  - Limits are stated as above.
- **Scope:** no production behaviour beyond A-D.

### Suggestions (Low, optional)
- `docs/v1.1.4/TUI-AUDIT-STATUS.md` (D-07 row, "D-07 review round 2 (15, …)"): `provider_status_plain.test.ts:227-459` holds 13 `test()` calls, and the 401/403 loop makes that 14 runs. The doc's own enumeration also adds up to 14. → Change "15" to "14".
- `cli/src/core/endpoint_identity.ts:38-39`: a URL that is unparseable only because of a non-numeric port, with a later `@` in its path (`http://host:abc/p@q`), is now cut to `http://q`. That is only diagnostic text, but it loses the host. → Optionally add it to the over-cut limit sentence in the D-07 row.

### Slop: Low
- The change is minimal and each fix has a test that exercises it.
- The old text-regex classification was replaced by a structured field.
- The cancel test replaced a test that accepted either outcome with one that asserts the exact path.
- The only blemish is the miscounted figure in the doc row.

I confirmed `origin/v1/v114-int-4` is that SHA. I read the code and ran no build or tests, so the author's tsc and cargo results are unverified by me.

CI on PR #481 at review time:
- **Passed:** CodeQL, javascript-typescript, and both `cloudflare-experiment` jobs.
- **Pending:** both `test-matrix` jobs (22 and 24) and Entire Gates. Merging needs all three at the exact head, with Entire at ACCEPT and zero findings.

### Consolidation checks
1. **File set (1).** `origin/main..int-4` is 53 files. Each branch's own diff, taken from its merge-base with int-3, gives the same 53 paths. (Diffing the branch tips directly against int-3 shows extra paths, because the branches sit on the older base.)
   - Line counts add exactly: `live_view.rs` is 170+50+73 = 293 added and 14+2+14 = 30 removed. `live_state.rs`, `live.rs` and the TS files add up the same way. The merge lost or invented nothing.
   - The three merge commits did not change anything outside the branches.
2. **`composer_hint` (1a).** Both changes survive.
   - The approval, checkpoint, "approval waiting", help, review and other overlay arms come first. The busy arms (`fit_activity_line`) and idle arms come after them. So `esc cancels` cannot appear beside an approval panel.
   - Narrow stall line: `fit_activity_line` falls back from the full line to `" no data N s · esc cancels "` and then to `" no data N s "`. At 40 columns the maximum hint width is 36, and the compact line is 28 characters, so it fits. With a draft typed, the budget is 21 and the shortest form is used.
   - Idle hints: both arms pick a short form (`⇧↵` or `↵ send · ctrl+j`) when the long one does not fit. The final truncation uses `saturating_sub`.
   - The test `a_silent_provider_is_reported_with_its_silence_and_the_way_out_and_clears_when_data_arrives` covers 40 columns, a typed draft, and tiny sizes (1x1, 10x2, 40x1). The test `narrow_composer_hints_are_complete_at_forty_columns` covers the idle hints.
3. **Status rows (1b).** D-01 to D-04 and D-06 to D-10 are present once each, in order. D-03 is only moved; the diff shows it as one removed and one added line. Every commit hash cited in the rows exists and is an ancestor of the head. Every test name I grepped for exists.

### Cross-branch interactions (2)
- **Click mapping.** `click()` uses `layout.row_entries` (entry index per screen row), not the widened `top` tuple. D-02's `(entry, line, row-in-line)` only feeds `anchor` through `note_layout`, so the widening does not touch D-10's click code. Both clicking and wheel scrolling work on the same entry indices.
- **Wheel and anchor.** `on_mouse` clears `anchor` whenever `scroll_back` changes, and the next frame re-derives it from `layout.top`. A click changes only `selected` and `focus`, so the view does not jump. The follow and "new output" logic is the same as before.
- **`LiveLayout` losing `Copy`.** The remaining call sites build the value and REDACTED it by move, or use `.clone()` in tests. I found no copy in D-02 or D-06 code. Rust accepts this only if it compiles, and the author's full `cargo test` run is the evidence for that.
- **Status rows.**
  - `Entry::Status` is replaced in place, so entry indices do not shift and the anchor stays valid. If the row's line count changes, the stored line and row are clamped, which the D-02 row states.
  - Clicking a status row does nothing: `selectable()` only accepts tool, diff and reviewable entries.
- **One timer, one stall notice.** An `Activity` event clears `stall`, and so do `AssistantDelta`, `ToolStart` and an idle session state. The status row therefore never shows beside a stall line. `the_wait_has_one_timer_not_two` pins the single timer.
- **D-07 draft restore.** It runs only on the `NO_PROVIDER` error, only when the composer is empty, and only for the matching prompt id. Mouse events and Ctrl+J are handled elsewhere. Ctrl+J lives in `on_composer_key`, which runs after the approval-overlay handling, so it cannot decide an approval.
- **Protocol.**
  - The new fields are all optional or defaulted: `notice.key` and `notice.detail` are `Option` with `serde(default)`, and `StreamStall` fields default.
  - TypeScript declares `stream.stall`, and the sidecar emits it.
  - Rust has a dedicated notice parse test. The `stream.stall` parse is covered only through the `live_view` test's `feed()`, not by a test in `protocol.rs` (see suggestions).

### Approval safety (3)
- Mouse events never decide an approval. They go through `on_mouse`, which does not touch the approval guard, and `is_typing` excludes them. D-10's own tests assert this.
- Opening an approval clears any held event and queued input in `pump_guarding_approvals`, which D-10 left unchanged.
- The wheel-run coalescing keeps the first non-wheel event in `pending`. If an approval opens in the meantime, `pump_guarding_approvals` drops that event.
- The run is capped at 512 notches.
- Nothing in the combination touches `seen_through`, the repeat window or the 1 s guard.

### Exit path (4)
- `end_session` runs modes off, drain, shutdown (3 s), drain, and then `tcflush` happens in the terminal guard's `Drop`. The drains are capped at 300 ms each, about 3.6 s in total, which is inside the 7 s stop-watchdog grace.
- The D-08 stall timer is `unref`'d (`client.ts:346`), so it cannot keep node alive.
- The NO_PROVIDER refusal is an ordinary error event and adds no timers.

### Counts and evidence (5)
- Rust `#[test]` counts in the nexus_tui sources: 308 on int-3, then +4 (scroll-stall), +13 (provider-firstrun) and +22 (input) give 347 on int-4. The count is purely additive, so no test was lost.
- A grep undercounts by 4 against the 312 from my earlier run on int-3, so adding the same 39 gives the author's 351. I did not run cargo myself.
- Evidence directories D-01, D-02, D-03, D-04, D-06, D-07, D-08, D-09, D-10 and `tui_input_capture.py` are all present.

### Docs (6)
- The `USER_MANUAL.md` section on the newline key and mouse is consistent with the D-09 and D-10 rows. It names `NEXUS_TUI_MOUSE=off` (also `0`, `false`, `no`, `none`), and the manual's Windows note and the D-10 row agree.
- It also discloses the split-report case (a stray `Esc`, which denies an open approval and cancels a running turn, as before). That is disclosed behaviour, not new.
- No row claims behaviour the combination does not deliver.

### Suggestions (non-blocking)
- `core/src/bin/nexus_tui/protocol.rs` — add a direct `parse_event` test for `{"type":"stream.stall",...}`, including missing fields and an unknown field, next to the notice test, since the notice type has one and `stream.stall` is only covered through `feed()` in `live_view`.
- `core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live_view.rs:93` — `fit_activity_line` adds `esc cancels` in its compact form even when the phase is `approval`, where the full line omits it. Real stalls only occur in the streaming phase and the approval panel arms come first, so it cannot appear today. Fix: add `if phase != "approval"` for consistency.
- There is no test that combines a stall with an approval or `provider_wait` and asserts the screen. This is optional.

### Good
- The `composer_hint` merge resolution keeps every approval arm ahead of the busy and idle arms. Hint fitting uses saturating arithmetic and is pinned by tests at 40 columns and tiny sizes.
- D-10's row-to-entry lookup stays independent of D-02's anchor tuple, so the two features do not interact.
- The line counts add up exactly across the merges.
