<summary>Agent "Independent review of A9 cgroup branch" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**REQUEST_CHANGES** at head `54f28245aadbc37f2616dbd7bd1f3913c7090a6d`. `origin/v1/v114-a9-cgroup` matches that SHA. The diff against base `4415cdc8` is 3 files: the new test, the evidence doc and FINDINGS.md. No path under `eval/`, `cli/src` or `scripts/` changed, and no other FINDINGS rows changed.

I found no host-safety blocker. The two evidence findings (F1, F2) are small doc fixes. The CI-fragility findings (F3 to F5) are the ones worth fixing before this becomes a required gate.

## Code Review

**Host safety: traced from code, no blocker.**
- **Limits before the command.** `eval/cgroup_supervisor.mjs:34-45` creates the `supervisor` and `payload` leaves, writes the five limit files and reads each back. It calls `fail()` and exits 125 on any write error or mismatch, before `report({cgroup})` or the spawn.
- **Joins the payload before exec.** The child is `sh -c 'echo $$ &gt; payload/cgroup.procs &amp;&amp; shift &amp;&amp; exec "$@"'` (`:53`). If joining fails, `&amp;&amp;` stops it and the hostile command never execs. Only trusted code runs outside the limits.
- **Memory and swap.** `memory.max=64 MiB`, `memory.swap.max=0` and `memory.oom.group=1` are read back, so the hog cannot reach swap.
- **Fork bomb.** The bomb cannot leave the cgroup, because fork inherits cgroup membership. `setsid` and double-fork change nothing, and `cgroup.kill` is applied at every exit path (`:57,93,124`).
- **Kill path for every command.** `runIsolatedProcess` has a wall-clock timer that calls `containment.kill()` (`eval/isolate.mjs:863`). `kill()` repeats `cgroup.kill` every 50 ms, sends SIGTERM to the supervisor and falls back to SIGKILL of the process group after 10 s (`eval/resources.mjs:144-155`).
- **Test process dies or is interrupted.** The supervisor stops the payload when its control pipe closes (`:96`). Test process death is covered even though the child is `detached`.
- **Test timeouts.** The worst case is bounded by the per-command timeouts (30, 20 and 4 s). A node:test 60 s timeout does not orphan anything.
- **Skip gating.** Skips happen without a delegated cgroup v2 user manager, `cgroup.kill`, `app.slice`, bwrap, prlimit or pgrep, and without the tmpfs fixture. When `NEXUS_EVAL_TEST_WORKSPACE_ROOT` is set, the same reasons fail instead (`:72`). The probe is a real contained `true`.
- **Other hosts.** cgroup v1, a container and a laptop without delegation skip with a printed reason. Root on a host without `user@0.service` skips, or fails if the fixture env is set.

**Findings:**

- **F1 (Low, evidence accuracy).** `docs/evidence/v1.1.4/a9-cgroup-verification.md:119` says "the test allows 0.25x to 1.5x", but the test has no lower bound. `evaluator_cgroup_limits.test.ts:235` only asserts `&gt; 0`. Line 140 of the doc states it correctly. Fix: change line 119 to "&gt; 0 and at most 1.5x".
- **F2 (Low, evidence accuracy).** `a9-cgroup-verification.md:107` says "all returned 0" for the host-side `/usr/bin/true` probes during the bomb.
  - The test records nothing: `note()` does not print `during` (`test.ts:208`). It only asserts at least 80% of at least 3 probes REDACTEDed (`:215`), and prints the array only on failure.
  - So "all returned 0" cannot be reproduced from the output.
  - Also, a `spawnSync` probe every 250 ms proves little about host effect. It would also REDACTED for an unconfined bomb that has not yet exhausted the user's pids.
  - Fix: `t.diagnostic` the `during` array, restate the claim as what was measured, or drop it. Evidence line 63 and the FINDINGS "host unaffected" wording should match.
- **F3 (Medium, CI fragility).** `test.ts:51` requires `app.slice` to exist at gate time, before the first scope. In CI the fixture env is set, so a missing `app.slice` is a hard FAIL, not a skip.
  - `scripts/ensure-ci-user-cgroups.sh` only runs one throwaway scope earlier. systemd may have stopped the slice by the time the test runs. I could not confirm that behaviour, so confidence is about 0.6.
  - Fix: run `resourceProbeProblem` first (it creates a scope), then check `app.slice`. Better, drop the hard-coded path and take the scope path from the supervisor report.
- **F4 (Medium, CI fragility).** `:177` and the other assertions require `memory.peak`, `pids.peak` and `pids.events` (`:176,192,212`). The gate only checks `cgroup.kill` (kernel 5.14 or later, `:46`).
  - A hosted runner whose kernel lacks `pids.peak` (a newer cgroup file than `cgroup.kill`) fails rather than skips. Confidence is about 0.5.
  - Fix: gate on `pids.peak` and `memory.peak` existing in the delegated cgroup, with a printed reason, or tolerate null in the fork-loop test and keep the hard asserts only for the bomb.
- **F5 (Low-Medium, flake).** `assertCleanedUp` (`:138`) asserts the 15 ms sampler saw the scope. That depends on timing.
  - A fast hog on a loaded runner could finish between ticks, and then the memory test fails with "the sampler never saw this run's scope".
  - The bwrap and prlimit processes carry the marker for the whole run, which shrinks the window but does not remove it.
  - Fix: snapshot `run-*.scope` entries under `app.slice` before and after the run and assert the diff is empty, or make the sampler requirement non-fatal.
- **F6 (Low).** `:296` does `t.skip` when the outer bwrap fails, including in CI with fixtures set. A runner where bwrap cannot create the namespace goes green without running the read-only cgroup test. Fix: when `NEXUS_EVAL_TEST_WORKSPACE_ROOT` is set, `assert.fail` instead of skipping.
- **F7 (Low, flake bounds).** The CPU upper bound is fixed at `quota*1.5` for a 2 s window (`:234`). Real usage is capped at the quota times the payload lifetime, and bwrap start-up and kill latency on a slow runner stretch that lifetime.
  - Quota 400 ms with the 600 ms bound is only about 0.5 s of slack at 20%.
  - Fix: bound on measured time, `usage &lt;= pct/100 * run.ms * 1000 * 1.1 + 100_000` (one 100 ms period). It never fails from slowness, and a lower bound is unnecessary.
  - The fork-probe bound of 80% of at least 3 is fine. Under load it is more likely to produce too few samples than a failed fork, so drop `during.length &gt;= 3` to `&gt;= 1` or lengthen the window.
  - The bomb asserts `timedOut === true` (`:210`). That fails if 16 bashes exceed 64 MiB and the group OOM kill ends the run first. Fix: assert `timedOut || oomKills &gt; 0`.
- **F8 (Low).** Control run: the evidence says `nr_throttled 0` (`a9-cgroup-verification.md:121`), but the test neither prints nor asserts it. Fix: add `assert.equal(run.final.cpuThrottledUsec, 0)`, or drop the claim.

**Cleanup.** Temp directories are removed on every path (`:129,250,274,302`). The test registers no shell cleanup of its own beyond the finally blocks, and scope cleanup is asserted. There is no leftover hazard from assertion failure, since the supervisor kills the cgroup regardless.

**Evidence and rows.**
- "9 of 9" counts one reporting test plus eight checks, and the doc says so.
- "41 refused forks" is `pids.events max 41`, which is accurate.
- Limits are honestly stated, including one host, kernel 6.8, CPU at two ratios, the 128 MiB tmpfs unchecked, and the CI run not done (I found no CI run on this branch).
- The pids gap is named precisely: `pidsMaxEvents` is dropped in `command.mjs`.
- The WP-1105 and A9 rows stay PARTIAL.
- The A9 row's pre-existing garbled sentence ("... (cgroup memory, pids, CPU) to these commands, both proven only with an injected process runner") was left in place. That is not a finding.

## Security Review

No vulnerability introduced. The hostile commands are argv-fixed constants. The marker is a `randomUUID`. Nothing untrusted reaches a shell or path. The test only mutates `process.env` temporarily and restores it in `finally`. The read-only-cgroup test's `-e` script is built from `JSON.stringify`-ed local paths and resources.

## Slop: Low

The two strongest reasons are that it reuses the production runner and supervisor report with real kernel counters, and that its fail-closed witness-file pattern is sound. Slop-level debris:
- `test.ts:233` has a dangling empty `//` comment line left from editing.
- The evidence doc is long and repeats the test file's header comment.
- The `any`-heavy typing is consistent with neighbouring tests and not charged.
- F1 and F2 are the only evidence-versus-test mismatches.

<result>**REQUEST_CHANGES** on `12712965adb232da6438d258e9e6e1c529e31403`, one medium finding (F3, below). There are no host-safety blockers.

I read the diff only and ran nothing. The "3 runs, 9 REDACTED" claim in the evidence doc is therefore unverified by me.

## Finding

**Medium: F3 is only half done.**
- **Where:** `cli/test/evaluator_cgroup_limits.test.ts:31, 83, 97, 156`. `APP_SLICE` is still hardcoded to `user@UID.service/app.slice`.
- **What changed:** The gate that required `app.slice` to exist was removed, but the scope path is still not taken from the supervisor report or a probe.
- **Trigger:** A CI or fixture host where transient scopes land outside `app.slice`. `listScopes()` and `scopesHolding()` silently return empty. The sampler never sees the scope, so `assertCleanedUp` fails with "the sampler never saw this run's scope".
- **Why it matters:** This now fails instead of skipping, which is the right direction. But the cause is reported as a vacuous check rather than a missing prerequisite. The snapshot comparison is also silently vacuous on such a host.
- **Fix:** Derive the scope directory from the supervisor report or from `/proc/&lt;pid&gt;/cgroup` of a probe run. Otherwise, name `app.slice` as a REQUIRED prerequisite in `requireHost`, which fails when `NEXUS_EVAL_TEST_WORKSPACE_ROOT` is set. Either way the failure message should name the cause.

## Checks you asked about

- **F1:** Verified. The doc says CPU usage is above 0 and at most `quota x run wall time x 1.1 + 100 ms`. The test asserts `final.cpuUsageUsec &lt;= (cpuPercent/100)*run.ms*1000*1.1+100_000` and `&gt; 0`.
- **F2:** Verified. The probe statuses are printed with `t.diagnostic`. The doc is restated to a weak signal and says the earlier runs' probe lists were not recorded.
  - Judgement: `forked === during.length` with no tolerance is stricter than before and can flake if one probe slips. The probes run outside the cgroup, so I think this is acceptable.
- **F8:** The control now asserts CPU usage within `0.5 x wall time x 1.1 + 100 ms`, not a throttle count. For a 0.3 s `echo; sleep` run this is not flaky, even on a slow runner, because the wall time scales the bound.
- **F4:** The else-branch is sound.
  - `pidsMaxEvents &gt;= 5`, `oomKills`, the EAGAIN stderr match and cleanup still run without the peaks.
  - A missing peak prints "NOT checked" and does not fail.
  - `requireHost` still calls `assert.fail`, not skip, when `NEXUS_EVAL_TEST_WORKSPACE_ROOT` is set and a required prerequisite is missing.
  - Residual: on a kernel without peaks, the CI fixture path goes green with only a diagnostic line. The doc says this branch was not exercised.
- **F5:** The `run-*.scope` snapshot comparison is correct.

<summary>Agent "Independent review of polish G3 branch" finished</summary>
<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**ACCEPT** at head `6d068437746b21ebfb8d2632724a5b62f433c005`. I verified that `origin/v1/v114-pol-g3` resolves to this SHA. I found no blockers: no Code Review defect and no Security Review vulnerability. I read the code only and ran nothing. Slop rating: Low.

## Code Review (low, no must-fix defect)

- **`cli/src/slash/workspace_commands.ts:~268`, `cli/src/clipboard.ts` (size check), `cli/src/sidecar.ts:1211`, `core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live_state.rs:~630` — near-limit copy can be refused after the reply says "asked".**
  - Trigger: raw text at or just under 74,994 bytes. The sidecar's `visibleControls` then rewrites each control character to a 3-byte glyph (`\r` becomes U+240D), and `&lt;U+00NN&gt;` for C1 controls. Redaction can also lengthen the text.
  - The TS check counts bytes of the raw text. The TUI's `request_clipboard` counts bytes of the sanitized text, so it refuses and pushes the "over the 74994" notice.
  - If the sanitized text also exceeds 74,994 UTF-16 units, `capText` appends `…[truncated N chars]`. The TUI still refuses, because bytes are at least chars. Nothing is cut and copied, but the reply and the notice contradict each other. The window is narrow, and the behaviour stays honest.
  - Fix: in `copy()`, run `formatSafeSidecarText` first and apply the byte check to the sanitized text. Alternatively, move the check to the sidecar boundary.
- **`core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live_state.rs:~1866` — on the crash screen with a non-empty draft, `Esc` quits and discards it.**
  - The screen says the draft is "kept", which is true only for a restart. Esc is the usual "cancel" key. Previously Esc also quit, but there was no draft then.
  - Fix: let Esc clear the draft first (a second Esc quits), or leave Esc unbound once a draft exists. `ctrl+c` still quits.
- **`core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live_state.rs:~1868` — a first keystroke `r` or `q` on an empty draft still acts as a command.** This is unchanged from before and is documented as a limit. A user typing a prompt when the sidecar dies can still restart or quit by accident. It is mitigated only by the draft-non-empty rule.

## Security Review (no findings)

- **Escape-sequence injection:**
  - The only text path into the terminal is `osc52_sequence`. Its payload is `base64(bytes)`, and the alphabet is `A-Za-z0-9+/=`, so it holds no ESC or BEL. The test counts one ESC and one BEL.
  - There is no chunking, and `?` is never emitted: the payload is never a bare `?`, and `base64('?')` is `Pw==`. Empty text is refused on both sides.
  - The sidecar has already turned control characters into visible glyphs, so injection is blocked twice.
  - The TUI's hand-written base64 matches the RFC 4648 vectors.
- **Size limit:** 74,994 bytes encodes to 99,992 base64 characters. With the 8 bytes of envelope that is exactly 100,000. tmux REDACTEDthrough wrapping is not needed.
- **Main-thread write:**
  - `write_clipboard` runs in `event_loop` after the sidecar events are applied and before `draw`. ratatui flushes each frame, so the two never interleave.
  - It writes no cells, so approval rendering and the opening guard are unaffected.
  - The D-10 restore paths are untouched. `SgrWriter` has no `Drop`, but flushes happen per frame and `ratatui::restore` writes straight to stdout.
  - A write error propagates with `?` and ends the loop, which matches the existing `draw` behaviour.
- **Redaction trade-off:**
  - A copy through the sidecar redacts credentials and makes control characters visible (`\r` becomes `␍`, so a CRLF diff pastes with `␍` at each line end). The tool path copies raw text.
  - It is intended, stated in the reply, and recorded in the status row as a limit. The sidecar protocol cannot carry unsanitized text, and a clipboard write is a plausible exfiltration sink, so I judge it acceptable.
  - Suggestion: add a test for the CR and redaction behaviour. `clipboard_osc52.test.ts` covers only ESC.
- **Version skew:**
  - The Rust protocol has no `deny_unknown_fields`, so an older TUI ignores the field.
  - The reply would then say "asked your terminal" when nothing was written. The two ship together, so I do not treat this as a defect.
- **No other emitter:** the line-mode REPL does not use the fallback; the status row states this.

## D-17, D-19, D-24 and CI risk

- **`SgrWriter` rewrite (D-17):**
  - It buffers until `flush` and rewrites only CSI `...m` sequences made of digits and `;`. `38;5;n` and `48;5;n` with n below 16 are rewritten.
  - n of 16 or more, `38;2;r;g;b`, `58;5;n`, `0` and `39` REDACTED through unchanged. OSC strings and truncated sequences are copied whole.
  - It is a REDACTED-through when not on 16 colours, so 256-colour and truecolor output is unchanged, and NO_COLOR emits no colour.
  - Per-SGR allocations happen only on Ansi16 terminals, which is acceptable.
  - `drop(try_init())` followed by `Terminal::new` is safe: the cursor is not hidden at that point, and the alternate screen is kept.
- **D-19 crash screen:**
  - `r` and `q` act only when the draft is empty. `ctrl+r`, `esc` and `ctrl+c` act as described. `ctrl+o`, `ctrl+x` and `ctrl+d` are inert.
  - `submit()` returns `None` while `Link::Crashed`. `fail()` calls `close_approval()` and clears the overlay, and `restarted()` calls `close_approval()`, so the #469 approval state is unaffected.
  - The draft lives in the in-memory composer, so it survives a restart but not a quit.
  - The rendering and tests cover 100x24, 60x16 and 40x10.
- **D-24 markdown:**
  - The heading glyph's width is included in the wrap through `hang`, and widths 0 to 100 are bounded. The old `▎↪` assertion was updated.
  - `cli/test/tui_surface_e2e.test.ts:172` only checks `includes("↪")`, so it is not stale.
  - The "extra leading space" non-reproduction is honest and consistent with the code.
- **Stale old-output assertions:** `rg` across `cli/test`, `core` and `docs/evidence` found no `38;5;15`, `48;5;0` or old "Your draft is kept" wording outside the updated tests. `tui_diff_e2e.test.ts` now expects `["ansi", n]`, and the driver helpers `tui_diff_driver.py:74` and `render_screens.py:46` already parse it.
- **`ACC-VER-08`:** it runs `dist/index.js eval --help` against the built `dist`. The diff touches no `cli.ts`, so it is plausibly a stale or unbuilt `dist` on the host rather than something this change caused. I did not run it.
- **Protocol:** `clipboard` is optional on both sides (`Option&lt;String&gt;` with `#[serde(default)]`). The Rust parse test covers it, the sidecar field-limit table is updated, and the projection test classifies the field.
- **Evidence and status rows:**
  - Hashes `ae799b70`, `c90fa6fe` and `7d70a122` exist and are ancestors of the head.
  - The rows are candid about running no real terminal and not running `tui_diff_e2e`. I found no overclaims.

## Slop: Low

The two strongest reasons:
1. The approach is sound and minimal, reusing `mouse_enabled` and the existing sanitization boundary. Tests are thorough and assert the claimed behaviour, including the hostile-text base64 case and the writer's frame ordering.
2. The only issues are small and isolated: the byte-count mismatch above, and a Rust and TS pair of duplicated constants (`OSC52_MAX_BYTES`) with no cross-check.

Relevant files, all under `/home/mike/nexus-wt/v114-pol-g3`:
- `cli/src/clipboard.ts`
- `cli/src/slash/workspace_commands.ts`

<result>**Verdict: ACCEPT** at head `aa94a08efdb939a69ffbcda10699d8d3ab161585` (`origin/v1/v114-pol-g4`, verified after fetch). I found no blockers and no new Entire-style defect at medium or high severity that needs a code change before merge. I only read code; nothing was built or run.

## Code Review
No high findings. Two low findings and one residual risk.

**Low**
- `cli/src/client.ts:~1930` (stream path of `chatCompletionStreamOnce`) — A stale comment now contradicts the fix. It still says "No resend here: a rejected fetch does not prove the gateway never admitted the request... Recovery belongs to the agent loop", but the `fetchResendingStale` call a few lines above resends, which is exactly that case.
  - Trigger: a reader trusts the comment.
  - Fix: reword it to "A resend happens only inside `fetchResendingStale` (stale keep-alive); any other rejection goes to the agent loop".
- `cli/src/client.ts:~898` — `replayable` can never be false, because every call site REDACTEDes a string or no body. The check also hides that the tokenize fallback URL `${root}/tokenize` (the endpoint with `/v1` stripped) never matches an endpoint prefix, so it never resends. That is correct and harmless but unintended.
  - Fix: drop `replayable`, or say in the doc comment that only URLs under a configured endpoint resend.

**Residual risk (medium-low, matches the author's own caveat)**
- `cli/src/client.ts:~895-903` — A reset before any response byte within 2 s is treated as "the server cannot have acted".
  - Trigger: a local server or gateway that reads the whole request and then crashes or OOMs 0.3–1.9 s into prefill on an endpoint that has answered before. Each of the 4 attempts then starts a new generation, so the same X-Request-ID is submitted up to four times.
  - Impact: cost and billing double-processing, and on a poison request, repeated crashes.
  - Contrast: the recovery controller treats this signature as `execution: UNKNOWN`, with a probe gate, a fresh linked id and `unknownReplays` accounting. The transparent resend byREDACTEDes that for up to 3 resends within 2 s. It is bounded, and the final failure is classified as before. `docs/v1.1.4/TUI-AUDIT-STATUS.md` D-20 documents the four-attempt case.
  - Fix, optional hardening: also require `cause.socket?.bytesRead === 0` and `bytesWritten` not above the size of the request, or add a per-endpoint counter that stops resends after N resends in a minute. That removes the repeated-poison case.

**Resend safety, point by point**

| Question | Finding | Verdict |
|---|---|---|
| Which failures trigger a resend | Only a thrown `fetch`, so response headers and stream data are never involved (after headers, `fetch` has already resolved). Only `UND_ERR_SOCKET`, `ECONNRESET` and `EPIPE` qualify; `TimeoutError` and `AbortError` are excluded. | Good |
| Aborts | `init.signal?.aborted` is checked after each failure, and the watchdog signal and operator signal are inside it. No resend after a first-byte or total timeout either. | Good |
| Request id and idempotency | `init`, including `X-Request-ID`, is reused, so the same id and headers are sent. A gateway that rejects a duplicate id with 409 would turn a harmless race into an HTTP error. | Note |
| Stall watchdog | One watchdog is created before the call, and the resend does not re-arm it. The 2 s window is well under the 120 s first-byte and idle timers and the stall notice, so the `stream.stall` semantics are unchanged. | Good |
| Recovery deadline accounting | A genuine outage is not hidden. Refused or unresolvable hosts are not resent, and 3 resends within 2 s all fail quickly, so the failure is reported as before. | Good |
| "Answered before" key | `hasEverSucceeded(origin)` uses the configured endpoint string from `this.endpoints`, the same key `markSucceeded` uses, so credentials stripped by `persistableEndpoint` are not involved. Each planner, coder and fallback endpoint has its own entry via `markSucceeded(pending()?.endpoint)`. | Good |
| Endpoint prefix match | `find` returns the first endpoint that is a prefix, so two endpoints that are prefixes of each other would use the first one's history. That is rare and only a nit. | Note |
| Streaming vs non-streaming | Both go through the same function. Anthropic/Responses native requests and embeddings are covered too. | Good |

**Probe merge-conflict lines**
- The edit touches `probeAvailability` in `cli/src/client.ts` at about lines 1612-1650, specifically the single `fetchResendingStale(...)` line at about 1624.
- Everything else sits at about 613-617 (consts) and 881-905 (the new method), plus the five call-site swaps at about 1576, 1736, 1925, 2217 and 2353.
- Any in-flight rewrite of the `probeAvailability` loop body or its `for (i &lt; total)` multi-endpoint logic will conflict only on line ~1624.

**Whole-suite risk (point 5)**
- Existing assertions encoding the old behaviour are safe. `inference_transport.test.ts` "reset before any response ... sent once" runs on a fresh client with no prior success, so there is no resend.
- `provider_recovery_agent.test.ts:83`, `provider_recovery.test.ts:312` and `team_convergence.test.ts:620` also use fresh clients, so `hasEverSucceeded` is false and a reset is sent once, as the tests expect.
- Mid-stream resets in `turn_semantics` and `cloud_providers` happen after the headers, so they are unaffected.
- The existing keep-alive reuse test is untouched, so it still protects reuse.
- In Rust, the existing `the_status_bar_renders_read_only_context_...` still REDACTEDes, since `context 1200/32768` is present with the percent appended. No leftover references to the removed `Activity.since` remain.
- The `if let ... &amp;&amp; limit &gt; 0` let-chain is fine under edition 2024 (rust-version 1.85.0).

**Test determinism (point 2)**
- `provider_healthy_server.test.ts` forces the race by counting requests per socket (`WeakMap&lt;Socket, n&gt;`) and destroying the socket on the second request. There are no sleeps. A reused pooled connection is killed and the resend gets a fresh socket.
- The `race.killed &gt; 0` guard ensures the test cannot REDACTED vacuously.
- The `unit-red-before.txt` shows 4 of 5 tests failing without the fix and the first-use test REDACTEDing, which is the correct shape.
- The only timing dependency is the 2 s window in the "exactly 4 chats" test, which is acceptable.

## Security Review
No vulnerabilities.
- The resend reuses the same headers and the `authorization(url)` computed once, with the same origin scoping.
- No credential reaches a different host.
- `fetchPolicy()` (`redirect: "error"`) is preserved.

## Slop: Low
The two strongest reasons:

2. Tests assert real behaviour. The Rust width, ordering, saturating (`saturating_mul`, `saturating_sub`) and "session id whole or not at all" checks, for 40..160 columns, are genuine.

The only debris is the stale comment and the dead `replayable` check above.

- **Segment priorities:** `ctx N%` is priority 3, ahead of branch (4), trust (5) and session id (6). `full` replaces `ctx` only if there is room after the keep decision, so it never displaces other segments. I computed 80 columns by hand: the 69-column bar includes `ctx 17%`, and the full form would be 87, which is over the room.
- **Widths:** All widths use `Span::width` (unicode-aware), and `limit &gt; 0` guards division by zero.

- **`esc cancels` in approval:** The compact form now omits it, and the test covers widths from 20 to 140.
- **Stall parse test:** Missing, partial and unknown fields are covered.
- **Docs:** Commits `0b5b2508` and `bf5899c2` exist and are on the branch. The before/after captures are consistent with the code. The documented limits (cost not shown, the 50x15 bar dropping the branch, the unchanged "cancelling… ctrl-c again" wording, 4 attempts for a poison request) are honest.
- **One detail to keep in view:** D-27 removes per-tool elapsed time for tool, approval and verifying phases. Only the turn clock remains. The author states this.

**Scope of the diff**
- It touches only `cli/test/evaluator_cgroup_limits.test.ts` and `docs/evidence/v1.1.4/a9-cgroup-verification.md`.
- `eval/` is untouched, so the production limit-before-exec path is unchanged.

**Checks**
- **Discovery:**
  - `discoverScopeParent()` runs a real `systemd-run --user --scope --quiet --collect -- cat /proc/self/cgroup`.
  - It reads the `0::` line, requires a `.scope` suffix, and uses `dirname` of the path as the scope directory.
  - That directory feeds the sampler, `listScopes`, `scopesHolding` and `assertCleanedUp`.
  - The probe's arguments match production (`eval/resources.mjs:104`), minus `Delegate=yes`. Neither sets a `--slice`, so the probe cannot land in a different slice from the real runs.
- **Lexical check:**
  - It rejects a `..` component and any directory not under `${USER_SERVICE}/`, with no existence check.
  - The `..` check runs on the raw cgroup string before `path.join` normalises it, so it does see the component.
  - Both failure messages name the cause (probe exit status, stderr tail and cgroup string, or the offending directory).
- **Skip or fail:**
  - The gate returns `{reason}` on a failed check.
  - `requireHost` then fails when `NEXUS_EVAL_TEST_WORKSPACE_ROOT` is set and skips otherwise.
  - The discovery step runs after the resource-contract probe, so a host that cannot run the contract is reported first.
- **ENOENT versus other errors:**
  - `readScopeDir` returns `[]` only for `ENOENT`, and rethrows everything else.
  - `listScopes` and `scopesHolding` both use it.
- **Sampler:** each timer tick is wrapped in try/catch and records the first error. `stop()` rethrows it inside the awaiting test, so it no longer surfaces as an uncaught timer exception. In `launch` (line 164), `sampler.stop()` sits in the cleanup path, so a stored error could mask the original one. That only affects which error is shown and does not weaken the check.
- **Vacuity:**
  - A wrong-but-lexically-valid directory can only be a different directory under the user manager.
  - For runs of 1 s or longer, `assertCleanedUp` asserts `sampled.scopes.length &gt;= 1`. That needs `scopesHolding(marker)` to find the run's scope in the discovered directory.
  - A wrong directory therefore fails with "sampler never saw this run's scope" instead of REDACTEDing quietly, so the &gt;= 1 s requirement is still meaningful. The only exempt run is the ordinary command at line 316, which uses `mustHaveSeenScope: false`.
- **Evidence doc:** I read the lines named in the doc at `1afe81aa`.
  - `ci.yml` lines 92 and 182 and `release.yml` line 107 are each `npm --prefix cli test`.
  - That runs `test:cli`, then `node scripts/run_tests.mjs`. That script runs `tsx --test --test-concurrency=1` over every `cli/test/*.test.ts` plus `eval/dataset.test.mjs` and `eval/package_runtime.test.mjs`.
  - The `make test` and `make test-ci` claims are consistent with the Makefile. `make test` runs `npm --prefix cli run test:cli`, and `test-ci` wraps `make test`.
  - The shared-host and parallel-run caveat is present.

**Suggestions (non-blocking)**
- `evaluator_cgroup_limits.test.ts`, in `readScopeDir`: `catch (error: any)` conflicts with the repo's no-`any` convention. Fix: `catch (error) { if ((error as NodeJS.ErrnoException).code === "ENOENT") return []; throw error; }`.
- The doc says "`make test`" runs the file through `npm --prefix cli test`. It actually runs `npm --prefix cli run test:cli`, which is the same runner minus `test:scope`. Fix: reword to "`npm --prefix cli test` or `run test:cli`".

<note>A task-notification fires each time this agent stops with no live background children of its own. The user can send it another message and resume it, so the same task-id may notify more than once.</note>
<result>**REQUEST_CHANGES** at head `61e0842c2baa538a59eafe7758553752123347a2`. `origin/v1/v114-pol-g2` resolves to that SHA after fetch. This was a read-only review (git, rg, reading); nothing was built or run, so every verdict below comes from reading the code.

There are no high-severity defects. The credential, redirect, abort and bounds handling for D-11 is sound. Three real defects remain.

## Findings

**1. MEDIUM — `cli/src/slash/session_commands.ts:238` (D-15).**
- Trigger:
  - Bare `/resume` takes the newest session in `listSessions()` whose id differs from the current one.
  - `~/.nexus/sessions` is global and `listSessions` does not filter by workspace.
  - `loadSession` goes through `workspacePathFromSessionFile` (`cli/src/session.ts:~520`), which throws "Session workspace does not match…" for another workspace's record.
  - `loadSessionLocked` catches that error and returns `null`.
  - `resumeSession` then reports `Session not found: &lt;id&gt;`, although the session is listed.
- Impact:
  - If the newest other session belongs to another project, `/resume` fails with a misleading message and never offers an older session from this workspace.
  - The change makes this far more likely, because the current session used to be the one picked. The trust boundary itself is unchanged and fails closed.
- Fix:
  - Filter candidates to `s.id !== host.sessionId() &amp;&amp; sameWorkspace(s.workspace, host.app.getConfig().workspace)`.
  - Alternatively, try candidates in order until one loads, and say "no other saved session in this workspace" when none does.
  - Add a test with a foreign-workspace newest session.
- The corrupt-session question, answered: a corrupt other session fails closed. `loadSession` throws, `resumeSession` catches it, and the user gets `ok:false` with the message. There is no half-load and no crash, but also no fallback to the next candidate.

**2. LOW/MEDIUM — `cli/src/model_catalog.ts:129` (`listedIds`, D-11).**
- Trigger: the session endpoint returns a model id containing a bidi or invisible character, such as U+202E, U+200B–200F, U+2028/9, U+2066–2069 or U+FEFF.
- Why it gets through:
  - The regex only strips C0 and C1 (`\u0000-\u001f\u007f-\u009f`).
  - Rust has no choke point for picker rows. `models.rs` and `render_models` never call `safe_text`; `diff_view::safe_text`, `approval_safe_text` and `visible_control` are used only for diffs and approvals.
  - The id is drawn in the picker verbatim, so a row can be visually spoofed.
- Related: stripping changes the id (`gpt\x07-4` becomes `gpt-4`), so the id selected may differ from what the server listed. Ids containing controls should be dropped, not "cleaned".
- Fix:
  - Reject any id matching `/[\p{Cc}\p{Cf}\u2028\u2029]/u`, rather than removing characters.
  - Apply `diff_view::safe_text` to the id and label in `render_models`.
  - The test at `model_picker_session_endpoint.test.ts:~140` currently asserts the stripping behaviour (`"evil[31mname"`) and must change with the fix.

**3. LOW — `cli/src/sidecar.ts:392-401` (`startFollowUp`, D-18).**
- Trigger:
  - Follow-ups g1 and g2 are waiting and the provider gate is down (`providerProblem` is set).
  - g1 is refused and returned to the composer as `cancelled`.
  - The `runJob` finally block still starts g2, which probes again.
- Consequences:
  - Each message pays a separate probe delay before it is returned.
  - If the provider recovers in between, g2 runs while g1 sits in the composer, which inverts the send order.
  - Before this change all of them were refused together.
- Fix: in `refused`, also cancel and return every remaining entry of `this.followUps` with the same message, then clear the list. Add a test with two follow-ups and a gate refusal.

## Merge risk with the D-07 `gatedTurn` rewrite
`gatedTurn` itself (lines 515–545) is not touched by this diff, so a rewrite of its body will not conflict textually. The overlaps in `cli/src/sidecar.ts` are:
- **Lines 389–404, `startFollowUp`:** this is the real conflict zone. The `gatedTurn(next.id, next.text, signal, { admitted, refused })` call and the `refused` hook shape are rewritten here. If D-07 changes the hooks signature or what refusal means, this hunk conflicts, and finding 3 interacts with it.
- **Lines 364–369:** the new early branch in `steer()`, immediately before the `queueSteering` call.
- **Other hunks, no overlap:** 232 (`provider.cancel`), 255–275 (`modelsList` and `modelLists`), ~1106 (`shutdown`).
- **Unchanged callers of `gatedTurn`:** `steer()` at 349 and `submit()` at 502.

## Verdicts on the specific questions

**D-11**
- **Credentials:** the client is built from the session `config` with no `providerId`.
  - `credentialFor(url)` returns only the explicit key bound to that origin, or the stored credential for that same origin.
  - No other provider's key is selectable.
  - Selecting the session row reuses `config.apiKey`, `credentialOrigin` and `authorizedDisclosureOrigins`, so it cannot switch credentials or endpoints.
- **Redirects:** `fetchPolicy` is `redirect:"error"`, so credentials are not forwarded cross-origin.
- **Abort and timeout:** `withTimeout` and `metadataSignal` both abort.
  - The `Promise.race` handlers prevent unhandled rejections.
  - Cancelled or older lists emit nothing, and the `finally` only deletes its own controller.
- **Two quick `models.list`:** the older one is aborted silently. That is safe because the Rust side has a single overlay.
- **Label, errors, current model:**
  - The label uses `originOf`, so it is host:port with IPv6 brackets and no userinfo.
  - Error text goes through `scrubUrls` and `redactSecrets`.
  - The session row does not write to the recents list.
- **Bounds:** non-strings are dropped, the 200-character and 1000-entry caps are applied, and the response body size is uncapped (pre-existing; only the 8 s timeout bounds it).
- **Limits to state honestly (suggestions):**
  - The session row only exists while `config.provider` is unset and `config.endpoint` is not the appliance. `setInferenceTarget` mutates the shared config, so after the user picks an appliance or cloud model the `-e` row disappears and cannot be returned to without a restart. The label "This session" slightly overstates this.
  - A `-e` session with `fallbackEndpoints` could list a fallback's models under the primary's label.
  - Whether the session row is the right moment to add `NEXUS_*`/env sessions is covered by the same limits; the status row already admits the env case was not captured.

**D-18**
- **Ordering:** follow-ups run one per turn in send order. A later steer queues behind waiting ones (`steer()` early branch). Cancel returns them separately, in order.
- **Persistence:** the queue is in memory only, the same as at the base. Shutdown stops starting follow-ups. The Rust crash path (`fail()`) returns steering that was never acknowledged as applied.
- **Approval safety (#469):** untouched. `restore_draft` only pushes a `Notice` into the transcript, so steering never becomes a decision key.
- **Audit expectation, judged:** this is partial, and acceptable only because the status row is honest ("FIXED (design kept, no silent merge)").
  - The audit's D-18 text complains of a silent merge and "no way to queue a separate next prompt", called "surprising and undiscoverable".
  - The change removes the silent merge on the sidecar side and adds labels and a notice.
  - The exact audit scenario (type two while waiting, Esc, Enter) still sends both as one message after a notice.
  - There is still no queue-a-separate-prompt path, and the owner's design decision is recorded.
- **Wording nits (suggestions):**
  - The `deferred` ack text "starts a follow-up turn as soon as it ends" is inaccurate for g2 and later.
  - The help line says "never merged", which is overstated for the Esc-to-draft case.
  - The notice says "split it first", but there is no split mechanism beyond manual editing.

**D-15**
- **Current session:** it is identified by id (`host.sessionId()` against the index `id`), so an unsaved current session is simply not in the list.
- **Ordering and symlinks:** order is file mtime, as `/sessions` shows. Symlinked session files are handled by the existing `statSync` and `workspacePathFromSessionFile` code, not changed here.
- **Unchanged:** the explicit id, `latest` and `--resume` paths.

**D-14**
- **`text_streaming` derivation:** it resets on a new phase or step (including tool and approval phases), and a delta only sets it when an activity exists. The tool, approval and `provider_wait` text is unchanged.
- **One-timer rule:** not violated; `inference` was already excluded from the timer.
- **Residual:** `writing` persists if a stream is discarded and re-run in the same step (a retry shows `writing`). This is minor and the author already lists the one-frame lag.

**Whole-suite and CI risk**
- No other assertion encodes the old wording: `during inference`, `queued for the next step` and `verifying (` appear only in the updated or new tests.
- Existing `cli/test/provider_connect.test.ts` catalog tests (around 713 to 1130): where the app's endpoint differs from the appliance endpoint (for example the pending local-endpoint tests), the session row now appears. Those tests filter by `provider === "appliance"` or use `some`, so they should REDACTED.

<result>**Head reviewed:** `3d3b521648540c14d4c0202531b8c816258a1da5`. Delta reviewed: `aa94a08e..3d3b5216`, read-only, nothing built or run.

**Verdict: ACCEPT** at `3d3b521648540c14d4c0202531b8c816258a1da5`. There are no Blockers and no High or Medium findings. One Low and three notes follow. Slop rating: **Low**.

### Blockers
No blockers found.

### Budget state machine
- **Mechanics.** The first failure calls `mayResend(key, false)`, which creates or refreshes the window and charges nothing. A failed resend calls `mayResend(key, true)` and charges 1. Resending stops when the charge reaches 3, so a single request sends at most 4 times, as before.
- **Poison case.** 4 + 4×1 = 8 sends for 5 rejected requests. The test's `5 + 3` matches the code, and the +61 s step gives 4 sends again.
- **Keying.** `key` is the matched configured endpoint plus the method, so POST and GET (probe) are separate. A probe success cannot clear the POST budget, and the probe-does-not-clear test is valid. The endpoint string is not credential-stripped, but the key never leaves the Map, so nothing leaks.
- **Growth.** The Map is bounded by the number of endpoints times the number of request kinds. `addFallbackEndpoint` is the only way it grows, and it is not driven by request volume. Expired entries are replaced on the next lookup and are not swept. That is fine at this size.
- **Window.** The window is fixed from the first failure, not sliding, and it uses the injected clock. The 2 s per-request window still uses `Date.now()` and is unchanged. Tests need no real sleeps for the budget.
- **Concurrent burst.** In a burst of N requests on N stale sockets, each socket is still busy with its own failing request. Each resend therefore needs a new connection, and each first failure is free. A sibling can only drain the budget if a resend lands on an idle pooled socket that is also stale. That needs spare stale idle sockets beyond the in-flight count, or a server that kills fresh-but-reused sockets, as the test server does. Even then each resend is capped at 3 and a later success clears the budget, so the effect is bounded. Acceptable.
- **Alternating server.** A success without a resend resets the budget, so a poison request still gets 4 sends per cycle. That is the same bound as the accepted `aa94a08e`, and the delta only improves things when there are no clearing successes. Acceptable and honestly documented.
- **Never-answered, abort, stream.** An endpoint that has never answered is never resent (`hasEverSucceeded` still gates it). Abort throws on `AbortError` or `signal.aborted`. Only a thrown fetch is resent, and the stream watchdog and recovery accounting are untouched. The 1948 comment change is correct.
- **Removing `replayable`.** The doc and the code both say a resend needs a URL under a configured endpoint. That is correct: bodies are all `JSON.stringify` strings, so the guard was dead.

### Suggestions
- `cli/src/client.ts:905` (Low). `if (key &amp;&amp; resends === 0) this.staleResends.delete(key)` clears the budget on any `Response`, including 5xx or 429, and also when the body dies after headers.
  - Trigger: a gateway that alternates a reset with a 502/503 gets a fresh budget every cycle.
  - Fix: clear only when `response.ok` is true or the status is below 500. This is optional, because the bound per cycle is the same 4 sends.
- `cli/src/client.ts:921` (note). The count semantic is "3rd charged failure stops", so effectively 3 extra sends per minute across requests, which matches the docs. Fine as written.
- `cli/test/provider_healthy_server.test.ts` concurrent test. It depends on sockets being fresh at resend time and on the server's kill-the-second-request rule. It looks deterministic, since resends open new connections before any response returns. If it flakes, increase N or assert on `race.killed` only. I could not run it.
- I did not verify that the concurrent and probe tests are red against `8541146a`. Reading the code, the probe test is red if the budget key ignored the method, and the concurrent test is red if the first failure is charged.

### Merge conflict with `origin/v1/v114-int-4`
`git merge-tree` shows one conflict, in `cli/src/client.ts` at about lines 1644–1691, inside `probeAvailability`.

On int-4 that is a plain `fetch(` today, which would silently drop the D-20 probe resend and break the probe tests ("six probes succeed", "probe-does-not-clear"). The method stays `GET`, so the key `&lt;ep&gt; GET` still works.

The round-2 work is in two commits, `0502429b` and `6945563c`. The second commit was added after the reviewer ran. It covers the reviewer's findings plus the unrelated edit in `startFollowUp`'s neighbour `resumeSession` described below.

## What changed

1. **D-15 `/resume`.**
   - Bare `/resume` now only considers saved sessions for this workspace and tries them newest first. An unloadable one is skipped and named with its reason, and a throw is caught the same way.
   - If none remain, the reply is `There is no other saved session in this workspace to resume`, with `Could not load: …` when some were skipped.
   - The workspace filter compares real paths, but the loader compares the path string exactly. A symlinked workspace can therefore REDACTED the filter and then be skipped by the loader. The status row says so.
   - Your three red-first cases are in `slash_resume_other.test.ts`: foreign newest falls to the older own session, only foreign gives the honest message, and a corrupt newest falls through with a note. I added all-unloadable and throwing cases. Four of the eight cases failed against the old code.
2. **D-11 picker.**
   - TS side: `listedIds` now rejects any id with a control, format (bidi, zero-width) or line-separator character, and any id with leading or trailing whitespace. Rejected ids are dropped whole, never cleaned into another id.
   - Rust side: every provider and model string the picker draws goes through `approval_safe_text`, so a bidi id shows as `&lt;U+202E&gt;`.
   - `model_picker_session_endpoint.test.ts` now asserts rejection instead of stripping. The new Rust test `the_picker_draws_no_invisible_or_direction_changing_character_from_a_provider` ran red first, at 40/60/100 columns.
   - I did not add a response-body cap. It is still bounded only by the 8 s timeout, and the status row says so.
3. **D-18 follow-ups.**
   - When the gate refuses a follow-up, every message waiting behind it is now returned at once, in order, with no further probes.
   - The test spies on the probe count, and the new test asserts 2 probes: one for the refused prompt and one for the first follow-up.
   - I did not run this test red against the old code; it failed at an earlier assertion before the fix, so treat the red evidence as partial.
4. **Wording.** The `deferred` ack now says the message runs as its own follow-up turn once the turns before it have ended. The help line says Esc returns unsent messages to the draft. The notice says `Enter sends the whole draft as one message; edit the draft if you want them sent separately.` Manual, evidence README and `after.txt` match. No assertion elsewhere depended on the old text.
5. **Docs.** The D-11, D-15 and D-18 status rows now state the cross-workspace behaviour, the new tests (including the "queues behind waiting follow-ups" test from `61e0842c`), the `-e` row disappearing after an appliance or cloud pick until restart, and the fallback-label caveat.

<result>### Blockers

- `/home/mike/nexus-code/cli/src/sidecar.ts:1062-1068` (`resumeSession`), Medium, confidence 0.8: moving `this.sessionId = session.id` after `emitHistory` leaves a split state when anything above it throws, and the next save overwrites the old session record.
  - **Trigger:** `restoreConversation` calls `restoreSession`, which replaces `this.messages`, the objective, the findings and the task state in `agent.ts:2121-2144`. Then `readArchive` and `restoreArchive` run, then `setActivePlan` and `emitHistory`. Any throw after `restoreSession` (an archive read hitting EACCES, a malformed message in `emitHistory`) leaves the agent holding the *resumed* conversation under the *previous* `this.sessionId`.
  - **Impact:** `restoringConversation` is reset in `finally`, so saves are enabled again. The next turn's `saveSession` (`sidecar.ts:1022`, `id: this.sessionId`), the progress listener at `sidecar.ts:133` and the archive sink all write under the old id. That replaces the record of the session the operator was in with the other session's conversation. Before this delta the id was set first, so conversation and id stayed paired and the worst case was a partial restore saved under its own id.
  - **Wrong comment:** "a throw above leaves the id as it was" describes the id, not the state. The `/resume` loop in `session_commands.ts` catches the throw and tries the next candidate, so the mixed state is reachable from ordinary use.
  - **Fix:** put the assignment back before `restoreConversation` (id and conversation then change together), or on any throw after `restoreSession` start a fresh session (`generateSessionId()` plus `restartConversation`) instead of keeping the old id. Add a sidecar-level test where `readArchivedHistory` throws and assert which id the next `saveSession` uses. The new slash tests only mock `resumeSession`, so they cannot see this.

### Suggestions

- `/home/mike/nexus-code/docs/evidence/v1.1.4/tui/D-18/README.md:8`: the README now quotes `after.txt` as containing "...; edit the draft if you want them sent separately.". `docs/evidence/v1.1.4/tui/D-18/after.txt:23` still holds the old "so edit or split it first" text. Either regenerate the capture or reword the README so it does not claim the capture says this.
- `/home/mike/nexus-code/docs/v1.1.4/TUI-AUDIT-STATUS.md:15` and `:21`: the D-15 row says "(8 cases)" but `slash_resume_other.test.ts` has 9 `test(` blocks. The D-18 row quotes the notice with a comma ("...one message, edit the draft...") while the shipped text in `live_state.rs:623` uses a semicolon.
- `/home/mike/nexus-code/cli/src/model_catalog.ts:142`: `\p{Cc}\p{Cf}` plus trim does not reject interior non-Cc/Cf characters that draw blank or confusably. These include an interior NBSP or U+3000, variation selectors, U+034F, U+2800, U+3164 and U+115F/1160.
  - The Rust picker now shows them as `&lt;U+XXXX&gt;`, so the TUI is covered. Any plain-text consumer of the id is not.
  - Optional fix: reuse the `approval_visible_control` ranges (a `\p{Zs}`, `\p{Mn}` and fillers class) in the rejection regex.
- `/home/mike/nexus-code/core/src/bin/nexus_tui/live_view.rs:3654`: the picker title (`Select model — {label}`) and the connect-flow labels at `3752-3849` are not REDACTEDed through `approval_safe_text`. They come from the operator's endpoint host or built-in provider definitions, not the server, so this is defence in depth only. `Fetch::Failed(message)` at `3680` is also unsanitised and pre-existing.
- `/home/mike/nexus-code/cli/src/slash/session_commands.ts:~238`: the `dirOf` memoiser (`resolved.get(dir) ?? (resolved.set(...), resolved.get(dir)!)`) is clumsier than the few `realpathSync` calls it saves. A plain `comparableDir` call per candidate is simpler.

### Status of the earlier findings (61e0842c)

- **Bare `/resume` not workspace-filtered:** fixed. Candidates are now filtered by realpath, older ones are tried when a newer one fails, and skipped sessions are named in the reply.
  - The filter is looser than the loader, which does exact string equality in `session.ts:547` (`selected.matches`). An exact-match session always REDACTEDes the filter (realpath is equal). A symlink-alias record REDACTEDes the filter, is refused by the loader, and is reported as skipped. No cross-workspace load is possible, and the status doc states the limit.
  - A throw from `resumeSession` is caught per candidate, but see the blocker for the state it leaves behind.
- **`listedIds` bidi and invisible characters:** fixed. Ids with `\p{Cc}`, `\p{Cf}` or U+2028/2029 are dropped whole, and padded ids are dropped, not trimmed. The test expects exactly `["kept-id","m0"]`, which I traced and it holds. The interior-invisible gap above is residual.
- **Picker render-boundary sanitising:** fixed for provider rows, model rows, the connect row and notes. The Rust test checks widths 40, 60 and 100.
- **Refused follow-up should cancel all queued:** fixed, with no double-cancel, ordering or lost-message path found in `startFollowUp`.
  - `refused` returns `next` first, then `followUps.splice(0)` in order, and the `.finally` then finds the queue empty.
  - A steer arriving during the gate probe sees an empty queue, `queueSteering` returns `no_active_turn`, it is pushed and acked `deferred`, and the refusal then returns it.
  - `cancel()` empties `followUps` before the abort, so the abort-triggered `refused(cancelled)` returns only `next`.
  - One residual wording case: a steer sent between `cancel()` and the abort's refusal is returned with "Cancelled before it was sent", which is harmless.
  - The existing code path where `probeEndpointProblem` rejects (INTERNAL, no ack for `next`) is unchanged by this delta.
- **Wording and doc nits:** changed in code and docs. The new nits above come from the evidence and status docs not being updated to match.

### Good

- Dropping a bad model id instead of cleaning it avoids a cleaned id silently matching another model, and the commit explains why.
- Follow-up refusal now returns every waiting message in order with one probe. The test counts probes and asserts the exact ack sequence.
- The `/resume` tests cover a foreign-workspace newest session, only-foreign sessions, a skipped bad candidate, all candidates bad, and a throwing candidate.

### Security, Slop

- **Security Review:** no vulnerability in the delta. The realpath filter does not widen what the loader accepts, and the render sanitising reduces terminal-injection exposure.
- **Slop rating:** Low. The two strongest reasons are the stale evidence text in `after.txt` and the misleading comment on the `sessionId` move, which hides the blocker.

<result>### Blockers
No blockers found. No open High or Medium.

### Suggestions (Low; none blocks the accept)
- `cli/src/delete_preview.ts:37` — `flat` is a second, weaker control sanitiser. `visibleControls` in `cli/src/file_diff.ts:233` already exists and covers U+061C, U+200E and U+200F, which `flat` omits. `flat` also shows U+FFFD where the sibling previews show control pictures. The header comment says a row can "never" be hidden by a bidi or invisible mark, which is slightly more than `flat` guarantees. The Rust choke point (`review_lines`, then `approval_safe_text`, which covers all of these) makes this non-exploitable for the TUI. Fix: apply `visibleControls` after replacing `\n`, `\r`, `\u2028` and `\u2029`, or soften the comment to say the client is the guarantee.
- `cli/src/delete_preview.ts:7-9` and `:60-66` — the header says it "never follows a symlink". `fs.opendirSync(abs)` follows a link, so a directory swapped for a symlink between the `lstatSync` and the `opendirSync` would have its entry names listed. The in-code comment admits this for the swap case. Impact is limited to names and counts on the operator's own screen, in a sub-millisecond window. Fix: reword the header to "does not follow links that exist when it looks".
- `cli/src/delete_preview.ts:23` — `formatBytes` is exported but used only inside the file, and no test imports it. Drop the `export`.
- `cli/src/delete_preview.ts:150-166` — the directory case and `ba7e9894:cli/src/tools.ts:53` (`forbiddenDeletion`) refuse `.` and `.git` paths before approval, so the preview's "workspace itself" and ".git" branches for lexical spellings are unreachable from the agent. This is harmless, since the preview fails closed and mirrors the engine. It is not a defect and needs no change.

### Checks you asked for
- **Escape and bidi injection:** `flat` makes C0, DEL, C1, U+2028/9 and the embedding and isolate overrides visible. The Rust `review_lines` then runs every row through `approval_safe_text`, which covers LRM, RLM, ALM, zero-width and tag characters. The sweep adds two delete kinds with `bad` injected.
- **Forged rows:**
  - Newlines in names, paths, link targets and file content are flattened.
  - Content rows always carry the `▎ ` prefix, so a content line cannot pose as a statement row.
  - The test at `delete_preview.test.ts:180` asserts exactly one genuine "Everything inside…" and one "Only this file…" row.
  - A lone surrogate from `slice(0, 160)` is neutralised by `encodeEvent` (`protocol.ts:213-215`), so the Rust parser never sees one.
- **TOCTOU, symlink and FIFO handling:**
  - Reads go through `realpath(parent)` and `lstat`. The file open uses `O_NOFOLLOW|O_NONBLOCK`, then `fstat().isFile()`, then at most 4 KiB.
  - Directory reads stop at the visit limit, and the truncation wording is exact at the boundary (test at `:167`).
  - A truncated private-key block is withheld after its BEGIN line.
  - The preview is advisory and the engine re-resolves at delete time, so a swap after the preview cannot widen what is deleted.
- **Mirroring `core/src/file_ops.rs` `delete_file`:** I traced these against `resolve_safe_path`:
  - Trim, using the Rust White_Space set including U+0085.
  - Backslashes treated as slashes.
  - An absolute path canonicalised, so a link is followed to its target.
  - A relative final link kept and judged for escaping the workspace, pointing into `.git`, or pointing at the root.
  - A directory refused without `recursive`.
  - A link to a directory with `recursive` removes only the link; `remove_dir_all` does not follow symlinks.
  - A dangling link ends in failure, as the engine does.
  - The only divergence is a lexical `.git` component reached through a `.git` symlink. The preview then says "Only this file is removed" while the engine refuses. That is the safe direction.
- **#469 guards:** unchanged. The delete review stays `kind: "args"`, so it keeps `seen_through`, the 1 s quiet period and the typing guard. The sweep covers both new kinds, with tokens that must reach the screen.
- **D-12 flag logic:**
  - `operatorDeclined` is reset at the start of `requestApproval`. It is set only in `resolveApproval`, which is reached only by the Deny key.
  - It is consumed in `onToolEnd` only when `denied === true`.
  - Abort settles the approval as false without setting the flag, and a test asserts that case.
  - A policy refusal never sets `denied`; a test at `sidecar.test.ts:512` asserts that.
  - `#[serde(default)]` handles older sidecars.
  - The Rust side clears the duration and substitutes the sentence.
- **D-21:**
  - The claim that only trusting is remembered is true: the untrusted and exit paths never call `trustStore.trust`.
  - The resize listener is added after the render setup and removed in `finish`, including the `setRawMode` failure path.
  - `wrapTokens` makes progress at any width, including a grapheme wider than the row.
- **Rust approval sweep count:** 17 is correct. The old count was 15 and the diff adds two delete kinds. `grep -c 'kind("'` in `approval_sweep.rs` at the head gives 17. The D-01 row says "over 17 review kinds", and no other doc carries the old number.
- **Docs and commit hashes:**
  - `ef3ff9d4`, `cb93c093`, `a3ddd3ff`, `0991bd00`, `4d32e2f0` and `4f9eb692` all exist in `69067429..ba7e9894`.
  - Each hash is cited against the right defect.
  - The D-13 row's "15 cases" matches the 15 tests in `delete_preview.test.ts`.
  - The tests named in the D-12 row exist, including the read-only fallback assertion at `sidecar.test.ts:512`.

### Good
- Fail-closed design: every `describeDeletion` branch either mirrors the engine or says "will be refused / will fail", and the outer `catch` never throws into the approval path.
- No protocol break. The preview reuses the `args` review shape, `denied` is an optional additive flag, and the old-sidecar and old-TUI paths are tested both ways.
- Tests assert what they claim: the 3 GiB sparse file, the FIFO, the exact-limit walk, the forged-row case, the U+0085 trim, and the cancel-is-not-denied case.
- The sweep extension keeps the #469 invariants machine-checked for the new content rather than relying on prose.

### Slop rating: Low
The two strongest reasons: the one duplicated sanitiser (`flat`) and a few comments in `delete_preview.ts` that narrate what the code does. Both are isolated and do not weaken the implementation. The evidence captures and the long status row follow the existing D-01 to D-04 convention.
